\begin{split}t^{A}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},\xi^{k}}\big)&=t^{A}\big(\phi_{u^{k}}\big)-{2{\rm i}\pi}\Big(\big(I+\mathbf{q}\mathbb{B}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{q}\Big)_{\alpha\beta}t^{A}(\omega_{\alpha})u^{k}(\omega_{\beta})\\
&\quad+\mathbf{q}_{k\sigma}\bigg(t^{A}\big(\phi_{\widetilde{u}^{\sigma}}\big)-{2{\rm i}\pi}\Big(\big(I+\mathbf{q}\mathbb{B}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{q}\Big)_{\alpha\beta}t^{A}(\omega_{\alpha}){\widetilde{u}}^{\sigma}(\omega_{\beta})\bigg)\\
&=\xi^{k}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}\big),\end{split}

(5.22)

where we have used expression (
5.16
) for the multivalued differential
\phi_{\mathbf{q},\xi^{k}}
in the first equality and the symmetry properties

t^{A}\big(\phi_{u^{r}}\big)=u^{r}\big(\phi_{t^{A}}\big)\quad\quad\text{and}\quad\quad t^{A}\big(\phi_{\widetilde{u}^{r}}\big)=\widetilde{u}^{r}\big(\phi_{t^{A}}\big),\quad\quad\forall\ t^{A}=t^{i,\epsilon},v^{i},s^{i},\rho^{k}

in the second equality. Here, as in (
3.30
) and (
3.36
), we have taken into account the fact that the cycles
a_{k},b_{k}
start at a marked point
P_{0}\in C_{g}
so that
\lambda(P_{0})=0
.

iii)
If
t^{A}=\xi^{k}
and
t^{B}=\xi^{l},
with
k,l=1,\dots,g
, then by assuming the sum over the indices
\alpha,\beta,\mu,\nu
, we have

\begin{split}\xi^{k}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},\xi^{l}}\big)&=u^{k}\big(\phi_{u^{l}}\big)-{2{\rm i}\pi}\Big(\big(I+\mathbf{q}\mathbb{B}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{q}\Big)_{\alpha\beta}u^{k}(\omega_{\alpha})u^{l}(\omega_{\beta})+\mathbf{q}_{k\mu}\widetilde{u}^{\mu}\big(\phi_{u^{l}}\big)+\mathbf{q}_{l\nu}u^{k}\big(\phi_{\widetilde{u}^{\nu}}\big)\\
&\quad-{2{\rm i}\pi}\mathbf{q}_{k\mu}\Big(\big(I+\mathbf{q}\mathbb{B}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{q}\Big)_{\alpha\beta}\widetilde{u}^{\mu}(\omega_{\alpha})u^{l}(\omega_{\beta})-{2{\rm i}\pi}\mathbf{q}_{l\nu}\Big(\big(I+\mathbf{q}\mathbb{B}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{q}\Big)_{\alpha\beta}u^{k}(\omega_{\alpha})\widetilde{u}^{\nu}(\omega_{\beta})\\
&\quad+\mathbf{q}_{k\mu}\mathbf{q}_{l\nu}\bigg(\widetilde{u}^{\mu}\big(\phi_{\widetilde{u}^{\nu}}\big)-{2{\rm i}\pi}\Big(\big(I+\mathbf{q}\mathbb{B}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{q}\Big)_{\alpha\beta}\widetilde{u}^{\mu}(\omega_{\alpha})\widetilde{u}^{\nu}(\omega_{\beta})\bigg)\\
&=\xi^{l}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},\xi^{k}}\big).\end{split}

(5.23)

Therefore, equalities (
5.21
), (
5.22
) and (
5.23
) yield that

t^{A}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{B}}\big)=t^{B}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}\big)+\log(-1)\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}(1-\delta_{ij})\delta_{t^{A},s^{i}}\delta_{t^{B},s^{j}},\quad\quad\forall\ t^{A},t^{B}\in\big\{t^{i,\epsilon},v^{i},s^{i},\rho^{k},\xi^{k}\big\}

provided that the cycles of the canonical homology basis
\{a_{k},b_{k}\}
start at a marked point
P_{0}
with
\lambda(P_{0})=0
. Furthermore, using the same relations we see that the functions
\big\{t^{A}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{B}}\big)\big\}
satisfy the following limiting behavior as the matrix
\mathbf{q}
is the zero matrix:

t^{A}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{B}}\big)\Big|_{\mathbf{q}=0}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}t^{A}\big(\phi_{t^{B}}\big),&\hbox{if}\quad t^{A},t^{B}\in\big\{t^{i,\epsilon},v^{i},s^{i},\rho^{k}\big\};\\
\\
t^{A}\big(\phi_{u^{k}}\big),&\hbox{if}\quad t^{A}\in\big\{t^{i,\epsilon},v^{i},s^{i},\rho^{k}\big\}\quad\text{and}\quad t^{B}=\xi^{k};\\
\\
u^{k}\big(\phi_{u^{l}}\big),&\hbox{if}\quad t^{A}=\xi^{k}\quad\text{and}\quad t^{B}=\xi^{l}.\end{array}\right.

(5.24)

Proposition 5.3

Let
\omega_{\mathbf{q}}
be the
\mathbf{q}
-differential defined by (
5.7
) and
\eta(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big)
be the corresponding Darboux-Egoroff metric (
5.8
) defined on the open set
\mathcal{M}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}}}
determined by conditions (
5.9
).

Then the following set

\mathcal{S}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}):=\Big\{t^{i,\epsilon}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big),v^{i}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big),s^{i}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big),\rho^{k}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big),\xi^{k}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big)\Big\},

(5.25)

where
t^{i,\epsilon},v^{i},s^{i},\rho^{k}
are the operations defined in (
3.21
) and
\xi^{k}
is given by (
5.20
), provides a system of flat coordinates of the metric

\eta(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big)
.

In coordinates (
5.25
), the nonzero entries of the constant matrix of the contravariant metric
\eta^{*}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big)
are as follows:

\begin{split}&\eta^{*}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big)(dt^{i,\epsilon_{1}}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big),dt^{j,\epsilon_{2}}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big))=\delta_{ij}\delta_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2},n_{j}+1};\\
&\eta^{*}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big)(dv^{i}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big),ds^{j}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big))=\delta_{ij};\\
&\eta^{*}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big)(d\rho^{k}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big),d\xi^{l}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big))=\delta_{kl}.\end{split}

(5.26)

Proof:
Due to Rauch variational formula (
5.5
), we have

\displaystyle\eta^{*}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big)\big(dt^{A}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big),dt^{B}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big)\big)

\displaystyle=2\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{\partial_{\lambda_{j}}t^{A}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big)\partial_{\lambda_{j}}t^{B}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big)}{\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}(P_{j})\big)^{2}}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}(P_{j})\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{B}}(P_{j})=e.t^{A}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{B}}\big).

We consider two cases.

First case:

t^{A},t^{B}\in\big\{t^{i,\epsilon},v^{i},s^{i},\rho^{k}\big\}
. Using (
5.21
) and properties (
3.37
), (
3.38
) and (
3.40
) we deduce that

\displaystyle e.t^{A}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{B}}\big)=e.t^{A}\big(\phi_{t^{B}}\big)=\delta_{t^{B},t^{A^{\prime}}},

with
t^{A^{\prime}}
being the dual flat coordinate of
t^{A}
in the sense of (
3.40
) and (
3.41
).

Second case:

t^{A}\in\big\{t^{i,\epsilon},v^{i},s^{i},\rho^{k},\xi^{k}\big\}
and
t^{B}=\xi^{l}
. We proceed as in the proof of Theorem
3.4
. Consider the multivalued differential

\Phi_{\mathbf{q},A,l}(P):=\frac{\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}(P)\phi_{\mathbf{q},\xi^{l}}(P)}{d\lambda(P)}.

Relation (
5.18
) implies that the jumps of
\Phi_{\mathbf{q},A,l}
are as follows:

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathbf{q},A,l}(P+a_{j})-\Phi_{\mathbf{q},A,l}(P)

\displaystyle={2{\rm i}\pi}\delta_{t^{A},\xi^{k}}\Big(\mathbf{q}_{lj}\phi_{\mathbf{q},\xi^{k}}(P)+\mathbf{q}_{kj}\phi_{\mathbf{q},\xi^{l}}(P)+{2{\rm i}\pi}\mathbf{q}_{kj}\mathbf{q}_{lj}d\lambda(P)\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+{2{\rm i}\pi}(1-\delta_{t^{A},\xi^{k}})\mathbf{q}_{lj}\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}(P);

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathbf{q},A,l}(P+b_{j})-\Phi_{\mathbf{q},A,l}(P)

\displaystyle=-{2{\rm i}\pi}\delta_{t^{A},\xi^{k}}\Big(\delta_{jl}\phi_{\mathbf{q},\xi^{k}}(P)+\delta_{jk}\phi_{\mathbf{q},\xi^{l}}(P)-{2{\rm i}\pi}\delta_{jk}\delta_{jl}d\lambda(P)\Big)

\displaystyle\quad-{2{\rm i}\pi}(1-\delta_{t^{A},\xi^{k}})\delta_{jl}\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}(P).

Now by using the residue theorem and the normalization property (
5.15
) of the primary
\mathbf{q}
-differentials
\big\{\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}\big\}
, we conclude that

\displaystyle e.t^{A}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},\xi^{l}}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{r=1}^{N}\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}(P_{r})\phi_{\mathbf{q},\xi^{l}}(P_{r})=\sum_{r=1}^{N}\underset{P_{r}}{{\rm res}}\Phi_{\mathbf{q},A,l}(P)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{2{\rm i}\pi}}\sum_{j=1}^{g}\oint_{a_{j}}\Big(\Phi_{\mathbf{q},A,l}(P)-\Phi_{\mathbf{q},A,l}(P+b_{j})\Big)+\frac{1}{{2{\rm i}\pi}}\sum_{j=1}^{g}\oint_{b_{j}}\Big(\Phi_{\mathbf{q},A,l}(P)-\Phi_{\mathbf{q},A,l}(P+a_{j}^{-1})\Big)

\displaystyle=(1-\delta_{t^{A},\xi^{k}})\bigg(\oint_{a_{l}}\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}+\sum_{j=1}^{g}\mathbf{q}_{lj}\oint_{b_{j}}\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}\bigg)+\delta_{t^{A},\xi^{k}}\bigg(\oint_{a_{l}}\phi_{\mathbf{q},\xi^{k}}+\sum_{j=1}^{g}\mathbf{q}_{lj}\oint_{b_{j}}\phi_{\mathbf{q},\xi^{k}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\delta_{t^{A},\xi^{k}}\bigg(\oint_{a_{k}}\phi_{\mathbf{q},\xi^{l}}+\sum_{j=1}^{g}\mathbf{q}_{kj}\oint_{b_{j}}\phi_{\mathbf{q},\xi^{l}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=(1-\delta_{t^{A},\xi^{k}})\delta_{t^{A},\rho^{l}}=\delta_{t^{A},\rho^{l}}.

\square

Remark 5.1

As in (
3.40
) and (
3.41
), the constant Gram matrix (
5.26
), which does not depend on the choice of the
\mathbf{q}
-differential
\omega_{\mathbf{q}}
, permits us to the following duality relations that connect the five families of operations
\big\{t^{i,\epsilon},v^{i},s^{i},\rho^{k},\xi^{k}\big\}
as well as the corresponding primary
\mathbf{q}
-differentials (
5.19
):

\begin{array}[]{cccc}t^{({i,\epsilon})^{\prime}}=t^{i,n_{i}+1-\epsilon},&\quad\quad\quad v^{{i}^{\prime}}=s^{i},&\quad\quad\quad\rho^{k^{\prime}}=\xi^{k};\\
\\
\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{({i,\epsilon})^{\prime}}}=\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{i,n_{i}+1-\epsilon}},&\quad\quad\quad\phi_{\mathbf{q},v^{i^{\prime}}}=\phi_{\mathbf{q},s^{i}},&\quad\quad\quad\phi_{\mathbf{q},\rho^{k^{\prime}}}=\phi_{\mathbf{q},\xi^{k}}.\end{array}

(5.27)

In particular, we observe that the degrees of the quasi-homogeneous and primary
\mathbf{q}
-differentials (
5.19
) enjoy the following duality relation, identical to (
4.12
):

{\rm deg}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}\big)+{\rm deg}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A^{\prime}}}\big)=1,\quad\quad\text{for all\ A}.

(5.28)

As two direct consequences, we can use (
5.26
) together with the already mentioned duality relations (
5.27
) to get the following results analogous to (
3.43
) and (
4.9
), respectively.

Corollary 5.2

The partial derivative with respect to
t^{A}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big)\in\mathcal{S}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})
(
5.25
) is determined by

\partial_{t^{A}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})}=\sum_{j}\frac{\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A^{\prime}}}(P_{j})}{\omega_{\mathbf{q}}(P_{j})}\partial_{\lambda_{j}}.

(5.29)

Corollary 5.3

Let
\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}
be one of the primary
\mathbf{q}
-differentials listed in (
5.19
) and defined by (
5.14
) and (
5.16
). Then, in the system of flat coordinates
\mathcal{S}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}\big)
(
5.25
) of the Darboux-Egoroff metric
\eta\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}\big)
(
5.8
), the vector field
e=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\partial_{\lambda_{j}}

is represented by

e=\frac{\partial}{\partial{t^{A^{\prime}}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}\big)}}.

(5.30)

In the following result we discuss the quasi-homogeneity property of the flat functions
\big\{t^{A}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{B}}\big)\big\}_{A,B}
. This is also similar to
(
3.56
).

Proposition 5.4

Let
E
be the vector field
E=\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\partial_{\lambda_{j}}
and
t^{A},t^{B}
be two operations from the set

\big\{t^{i,\alpha},v^{i},s^{i},\rho^{k},\xi^{k}\big\}
. Then

E.t^{A}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{B}}\big)=(d_{A}+d_{B})t^{A}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{B}}\big)+\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\delta_{t^{A},s^{i}}\delta_{t^{B},s^{j}}\Big(\frac{1}{n_{0}+1}+\delta_{ij}\frac{1}{n_{i}+1}\Big).

(5.31)

Proof:

Assume that
t^{A},t^{B}\in\big\{t^{i,\epsilon},v^{i},s^{i},\rho^{k}\big\}
. By using (
5.21
), (
5.1
) and applying twice (
3.56
), we see that

\displaystyle E.t^{A}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{B}}\big)

\displaystyle=E.t^{A}\big(\phi_{t^{B}}\big)-{2{\rm i}\pi}\Big(\big(I+\mathbf{q}\mathbb{B}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{q}\Big)_{\alpha\beta}\Big(E.\big[t^{A}(\omega_{\alpha})t^{B}(\omega_{\beta})\big]\Big)

\displaystyle=(d_{A}+d_{B})t^{A}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{B}}\big)+\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\delta_{t^{A},s^{i}}\delta_{t^{B},s^{j}}\Big(\frac{1}{n_{0}+1}+\delta_{ij}\frac{1}{n_{i}+1}\Big).

Let us now deal with the case where
t^{A}\in\big\{t^{i,\epsilon},v^{i},s^{i},\rho^{k},\xi^{k}\big\}
and
t^{B}=\xi^{l}
. By studying the analytic properties of the multivalued differential

\lambda(P)\Phi_{\mathbf{q},A,l}(P):=\lambda(P)\frac{\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}(P)\phi_{\mathbf{q},\xi^{l}}(P)}{d\lambda(P)}

(its jumps and its poles) inside a fundamental polygon
F_{g}
associated with the surface
C_{g}
, in order to arrive at (
5.31
), it suffices to use the equality

\displaystyle E.t^{A}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},\xi^{l}}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{r=1}^{N}\lambda_{j}\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}(P_{r})\phi_{\mathbf{q},\xi^{l}}(P_{r})=\sum_{r=1}^{N}\underset{P_{r}}{{\rm res}}\Big(\lambda(P)\Phi_{\mathbf{q},A,l}(P)\Big)

(the first equality follows from Rauch variational formulas (
5.5
)) and apply the residue theorem and proceed as in the proof of formula (
8.12
).

\square

More generally, if
\omega_{\mathbf{q}}
is a quasi-homogeneous
\mathbf{q}
-differential of degree
d_{0}
in the sense of Definition
5.1
and
t^{A}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})
be one of the operations listed in (
5.25
), then the action of the vector field
E=\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\partial_{\lambda_{j}}
on the function
t^{A}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})
is determined by

E.t^{A}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})=(d_{0}+d_{A})t^{A}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})+r_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}},\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}},

(5.32)

where
d_{A}
is the degree of
\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}
and
r_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}},\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}}
is a constant depending on
\omega_{\mathbf{q}}
and
\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}
.

As examples, when
\omega_{\mathbf{q}}
is among the following quasi-homogeneous
\mathbf{q}
-differentials

\underset{\infty^{i}}{\rm res}\ \lambda(Q)^{1+\frac{\beta}{n_{i}+1}}W_{\mathbf{q}}(P,Q),\quad\quad\oint_{a_{k}}\big(\lambda(Q)\big)^{\sigma}W_{\mathbf{q}}(P,Q),\quad\quad\oint_{b_{k}}\big(\lambda(Q)\big)^{\nu}W_{\mathbf{q}}(P,Q),

where
\beta,\sigma,\nu
are positive integers, then the constants
r_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}},\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}}
in (
5.32
) are zero.

5.2
Prepotentials of the
\mathbf{q}
-deformed Frobenius manifold structures

Consider a quasi-homogeneous
\mathbf{q}
-differential
\omega_{\mathbf{q}}
of degree
d_{0}\geq 0
(see Definition
5.1
) such that
\omega_{\mathbf{q}}(P_{j})\neq 0
for all
j=1,\dots,N
. Then, the open set
\mathcal{M}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}}}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{g,L}(n_{0},\dots,n_{m})
determined by conditions (
5.9
) can be endowed with a Frobenius manifold structure (in the sense of Definition
2.2
) described by

•

the flat metric
\eta\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}\big)
(
5.8
) and the corresponding system of flat coordinates (
5.25
);

•

the multiplication low “
\circ
”:
\partial_{\lambda_{i}}\circ\partial_{\lambda_{j}}=\delta_{ij}\partial_{\lambda_{i}}
;

•

the unit and Euler vector fields
e=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\partial_{\lambda_{j}}
and
E=\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\partial_{\lambda_{j}}
.

Note that the symmetric 3-tensor
\mathbf{c}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}}}
defined by

\mathbf{c}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}}}(x,y,z):=\eta(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})(x\circ y,z)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j}x_{j}y_{j}z_{j}\omega_{\mathbf{q}}(P_{j})^{2},

with
x=\sum_{j}x_{j}\partial_{\lambda_{j}}
,
y=\sum_{j}y_{j}\partial_{\lambda_{j}}
and
z=\sum_{j}z_{j}\partial_{\lambda_{j}}
, has the following form with respect to flat coordinates (
5.25
):

\begin{split}\mathbf{c}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}}}\big(\partial_{t^{A}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})},\partial_{t^{B}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})},\partial_{t^{C}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})}\big)&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A^{\prime}}}(P_{j})\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{B^{\prime}}}(P_{j})\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{C^{\prime}}}(P_{j})}{\omega_{\mathbf{q}}(P_{j})}=\partial_{t^{C}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})}t^{A^{\prime}}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{B^{\prime}}}\big),\end{split}

(5.33)

where the second equality is an immediate consequence of (
5.29
) and Rauch formulas (
5.5
) for the
\mathbf{q}
-bidifferential
W_{\mathbf{q}}(P,Q)
.

As consequence, in the system of flat coordinates (
5.25
), the multiplication
``\circ"
is given in terms of the tensor (
5.33
) as follows:

\partial_{t^{A}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})}\circ\partial_{t^{B}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})}=\sum_{C}\mathbf{c}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}}}\big(\partial_{t^{A}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})},\partial_{t^{B}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})},\partial_{t^{C^{\prime}}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})}\big)\partial_{t^{C}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})}.

Now, by (
5.28
), (
5.33
) and a direct adaptation of the proof of formulas (
4.19
) and (
4.27
), we arrive at formula (
5.34
) below, which offers a new approach to calculate the prepotential associated with a deformed Hurwitz-Frobenius manifold structure (where the unity is not necessarily flat). Note that the method suggested in
[
42
]
uses an analogue of the Dubrovin bilinear pairing.

Theorem 5.1

Let
\omega_{\mathbf{q}}
be a quasi-homogeneous
\mathbf{q}
-differential of degree
d_{0}\geq 0
,
\big\{t^{A}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})\big\}
be the set of flat coordinates (
5.25
) of the flat metric
\eta(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})
(
5.8
) and
\mathbf{c}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}}}
be the symmetric tensor (
5.33
).

Consider the function
\mathbf{F}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}}}
defined by

\begin{split}&\mathbf{F}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}}}=\frac{1}{2(1+d_{0})}\sum_{A,B}\frac{\big((d_{0}+d_{A})t^{A}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})+r_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}},\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}}\big)\big((d_{0}+d_{B})t^{B}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})+r_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}},\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{B}}}\big)}{1+d_{0}+d_{A^{\prime}}}t^{A^{\prime}}(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{B^{\prime}}})\\
&\quad+\frac{\log(-1)}{4}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}v^{i}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})v^{j}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})-\frac{3}{4(1+d_{0})}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\bigg(\frac{1}{n_{0}+1}+\delta_{ij}\frac{1}{n_{i}+1}\bigg)v^{i}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})v^{j}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}),\end{split}

(5.34)

with
v^{i}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})
being the function
v^{i}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}):=\underset{\infty^{i}}{{\rm res}\ }\lambda(P)\omega_{\mathbf{q}}(P)
.

Then we have the following results:

1.

The function
\mathbf{F}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}}}
is a prepotential of the Frobenius manifold structure induced by
\omega_{\mathbf{q}}
, i.e.

\partial_{t^{A}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})}\partial_{t^{B}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})}\partial_{t^{C}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})}\mathbf{F}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}}}=\mathbf{c}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}}}\big(\partial_{t^{A}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})},\partial_{t^{B}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})},\partial_{t^{C}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})}\big).

2.

The function
\mathbf{F}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}}}
is quasi-homogeneous of degree
2+2d_{0}
in the sense of (
2.4
), with

E.\mathbf{F}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}}}=2(1+d_{0})\mathbf{F}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\bigg(\frac{1}{n_{0}+1}+\delta_{ij}\frac{1}{n_{i}+1}\bigg)v^{i}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})v^{j}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}}),

(5.35)

and
E
being the Euler vector field

E=\sum_{A}\Big((d_{0}+d_{A})t^{A}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})+r_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}},\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{A}}}\Big)\partial_{t^{A}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})}.

(5.36)

3.

The Hessian matrix of
\mathbf{F}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}}}
does not depend on the chosen quasi-homogeneous
\mathbf{q}
-differential
\omega_{\mathbf{q}}
and is determined by

\partial_{t^{A}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})}\partial_{t^{B}(\omega_{\mathbf{q}})}\mathbf{F}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q}}}=t^{A^{\prime}}(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{B^{\prime}}})-\frac{\log(-1)}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}(1-\delta_{ij})\delta_{t^{A},v^{i}}\delta_{t^{B},v^{j}}.

(5.37)

As in Section 4.1.5, we are going to pay attention to quasi-homogeneous solutions to the WDVV equations induced by the normalized holomorphic
\mathbf{q}
-differentials

\big\{\omega_{\mathbf{q},j}=\phi_{\mathbf{q},\rho^{j}}\big\}
, introduced in (
5.10
).

Basing on formula (
5.34
), we can extend all the results of Section 4.4.5. to the
\mathbf{q}
-deformation setting. However, we plan to concentrate on the
\mathbf{q}
-analogues of the solutions in (
4.39
) and (
4.42
).

For each
l=1,\dots,g
, the holomorphic
\mathbf{q}
-differential
\omega_{\mathbf{q},l}
is
e
-primary (by Proposition
5.2
) and quasi-homogeneous of degree 0. Thus, the flat coordinates
t^{A}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q},l}\big)
are eigenfunctions of the Euler vector field
E
and (
5.31
) becomes as follows:

E.t^{A}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q},l}\big)=d_{A}t^{A}\big(\omega_{\mathbf{q},l}\big),\quad\quad\forall\ t^{A}\in\big\{t^{i,\epsilon},v^{i},s^{i},\rho^{k},\xi^{k}\big\}.

In addition, according to (
5.34
), the prepotential
\mathbf{F}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q},l}}
is determined by

\begin{split}\mathbf{F}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q},l}}&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{A,B}\frac{d_{A}d_{B}}{1+d_{A^{\prime}}}t^{A}(\omega_{\mathbf{q},l})t^{B}(\omega_{\mathbf{q},l})t^{A^{\prime}}(\phi_{\mathbf{q},t^{B^{\prime}}})+\frac{\log(-1)}{4}\sum_{j,k=1,j\neq k}^{m}v^{j}(\omega_{\mathbf{q},l})v^{k}(\omega_{\mathbf{q},l})\\
&\quad-\frac{3}{4}\sum_{j,k=1}^{m}\bigg(\frac{1}{n_{0}+1}+\delta_{jk}\frac{1}{n_{j}+1}\bigg)v^{j}(\omega_{\mathbf{q},l})v^{k}(\omega_{\mathbf{q},l}).\end{split}

(5.38)

In order to extend formulas (
4.39
) and (
4.42
) to the framework of
\mathbf{q}
-deformation, let us start by expressing the functions

s^{j}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},s^{i}}\big):=p.v.\int_{\infty^{0}}^{\infty^{j}}\phi_{\mathbf{q},s^{i}},\quad\quad i,j=1,\dots,m,

by means of flat coordinates of the flat metric
\eta(\omega_{\mathbf{q},l})
(
5.8
) induced by the normalized holomorphic
\mathbf{q}
-differentials. Here

\phi_{\mathbf{q},s^{i}}
is an Abelian differential of the third kind normalized by (
5.13
), where the simple poles occur at
\infty^{i}
and
\infty^{0}
and the principal value is the same as in Theorem
3.3
.
The functions
s^{j}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},s^{i}}\big)
are the
\mathbf{q}
-analogue of those obtained in (
3.32
).

Proposition 5.5

Let
\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}
be the
\mathbf{q}
-Riemann matrix (
5.12
) and
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{q}}(P)
be the following
\mathbf{q}
-Abel map:

\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{q}}(P):=\Big(\int^{P}\omega_{\mathbf{q},1},\dots,\int^{P}\omega_{\mathbf{q},g}\Big)^{T},

(5.39)

where
\omega_{\mathbf{q},1},\dots,\omega_{\mathbf{q},g}
are the holomorphic
\mathbf{q}
-differentials defined by (
5.10
).

If
i\neq j
, then

\begin{split}s^{j}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},s^{i}}\big)&=\log\Theta_{\Delta}\left(\big(I-\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\mathbf{q}\big)^{-1}\big[\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{q}}(\infty^{j})-\mathcal{A}\mathbf{{}_{q}}(\infty^{i})]\Big|\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\big(I-\mathbf{q}\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\big)^{-1}\right)\\
&\quad-\log\Theta_{\Delta}\left((\big(I-\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\mathbf{q}\big)^{-1}\big[\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{q}}(\infty^{j})-\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{q}}(\infty^{0})\Big]\Big|\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\big(I-\mathbf{q}\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\big)^{-1}\right)\\
&\quad-\log\Theta_{\Delta}\left(\big(I-\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\mathbf{q}\big)^{-1}\big[\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{q}}(\infty^{i})-\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{q}}(\infty^{0})]\Big|\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\big(I-\mathbf{q}\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\big)^{-1}\right)\\
&\quad+\log\left(\sum_{k=1}^{g}\big(I-\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\mathbf{q}\big)^{k\alpha}\omega_{\mathbf{q},\alpha}(\infty^{0})\partial_{z_{k}}\Theta_{\Delta}\Big(0\Big|\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\big(I-\mathbf{q}\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\big)^{-1}\Big)\right)\\
&\quad-{2{\rm i}\pi}\big(\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{q}}(\infty^{i})-\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{q}}(\infty^{0})\big)^{T}\mathbf{q}\big(I-\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\mathbf{q}\big)^{-1}\big(\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{q}}(\infty^{j})-\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{q}}(\infty^{0})\big)-\log(-1).\end{split}

(5.40)

When
i=j
, we have

\begin{split}s^{i}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},s^{i}}\big)&=\log\left(\sum_{k=1}^{g}\big(I-\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\mathbf{q}\big)^{k\alpha}\omega_{\mathbf{q},\alpha}(\infty^{i})\partial_{z_{k}}\Theta_{\Delta}\Big(0\Big|\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\big(I-\mathbf{q}\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\big)^{-1}\Big)\right)\\
&\quad+\log\left(\sum_{k=1}^{g}\big(I-\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\mathbf{q}\big)^{k\alpha}\omega_{\mathbf{q},\alpha}(\infty^{0})\partial_{z_{k}}\Theta_{\Delta}\Big(0\Big|\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\big(I-\mathbf{q}\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\big)^{-1}\Big)\right)\\
&\quad-2\log\left(\Theta_{\Delta}\Big(\big(I-\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\mathbf{q}\big)^{-1}\big[\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{q}}(\infty^{i})-\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{q}}(\infty^{0})\big]\Big|\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\big(I-\mathbf{q}\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\big)^{-1}\Big)\right)\\
&\quad-{2{\rm i}\pi}\big(\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{q}}(\infty^{i})-\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{q}}(\infty^{0})\big)^{T}\mathbf{q}\big(I-\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\mathbf{q}\big)^{-1}\big(\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{q}}(\infty^{i})-\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{q}}(\infty^{0})\big)-\log(-1).\end{split}

(5.41)

Here, we have assumed the sum over the repeated indices
\alpha=1,\dots,g
.

Proof:
Due to (
5.12
) we have

\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}=\big(I+\mathbb{B}\mathbf{q}\big)^{-1}\mathbb{B},\quad\quad\big(I+\mathbb{B}\mathbf{q}\big)^{-1}=I-\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\mathbf{q}\quad\quad\text{and}\quad\quad\mathbb{B}=\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\big(I-\mathbf{q}\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\big)^{-1}.

(5.42)

This and (
5.10
) enable us to write

\omega_{\mathbf{q},k}(P)=\big(I+\mathbb{B}\mathbf{q}\big)^{k\alpha}\omega_{\alpha}(P)=\big(I-\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\mathbf{q}\big)_{k\alpha}\omega_{\alpha}(P).

(5.43)

In particular, the usual Abel map and the introduced
\mathbf{q}
-Abel map (
5.39
) are related by

\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{q}}(P)=\big(I+\mathbb{B}\mathbf{q}\big)^{-1}\mathcal{A}(P)=\big(I-\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\mathbf{q}\big)\mathcal{A}(P).

(5.44)

Finally, recalling expressions (
3.32
) for the function
s_{ij}
obtained in Theorem
3.3
, we conclude that relations (
5.40
) and (
5.41
) follow from the following equality (due to (
5.21
))

\displaystyle s^{j}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},s^{i}}\big)

\displaystyle=s_{ij}-{2{\rm i}\pi}\Big(\big(I+\mathbf{q}\mathbb{B}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{q}\Big)_{\alpha\beta}s^{i}(\omega_{\alpha})s^{j}(\omega_{\beta})

\displaystyle=s_{ij}-{2{\rm i}\pi}\big(\mathcal{A}(\infty^{i})-\mathcal{A}(\infty^{0})\big)^{T}\Big(\big(I+\mathbf{q}\mathbb{B}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{q}\Big)\big(\mathcal{A}(\infty^{j})-\mathcal{A}(\infty^{0})\big)

and the preceding formulas (
5.42
), (
5.43
) and (
5.44
).

\square

Let us now consider the
(2m+2g)
-dimensional Hurwitz space
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{g,m+1}(0,\dots,0)
of ramified coverings
(C_{g},\lambda)
having
m+1
simple poles
\infty^{0},\dots,\infty^{m}
. In this case, prepotential (
5.38
) takes the following form:

\begin{split}\mathbf{F}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q},l}}&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}v^{i}(\omega_{\mathbf{q},l})v^{j}(\omega_{\mathbf{q},l})s^{j}(\phi_{\mathbf{q},s^{i}})+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{k=1}^{g}v^{i}(\omega_{\mathbf{q},l})\xi^{k}(\omega_{\mathbf{q},l})s^{i}(\omega_{\mathbf{q},k})\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{4{\rm i}\pi}\sum_{j,k=1}^{g}\xi^{k}(\omega_{\mathbf{q},l})\xi^{j}(\omega_{\mathbf{q},l})\big(\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{q}}\big)_{jk}+\frac{\log(-1)}{4}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}v^{i}(\omega_{\mathbf{q},l})v^{j}(\omega_{\mathbf{q},l})\\
&\quad-\frac{3}{4}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\big(1+\delta_{ij}\big)v^{i}(\omega_{\mathbf{q},l})v^{j}(\omega_{\mathbf{q},l}).\end{split}

(5.45)

In particular, this formula and expressions (
5.40
) and (
5.41
) for the functions
s^{i}(\phi_{\mathbf{q},s^{j}})
allow us to arrive at the
\mathbf{q}
-analogue of formula (
4.39
). Moreover, we observe that the function
\mathbf{F}_{\omega_{\mathbf{q},j}}
in (
5.45
) only depends on flat coordinates corresponding to the
g
holomorphic
\mathbf{q}
-differentials (
5.10
). Accordingly, (
5.45
) turns out to be a new explicit family of examples (parameterized by the values of the complex number
\mathbf{q}
) of genus one quasi-homogeneous solutions to the WDVV equations (
1.6
).

In the rest of this section, we are going to focus on this example. Denote by
\omega_{0}
be the (genus one) holomorphic differential normalized by

\oint_{a}\omega_{0}=1,\quad\quad\oint_{b}\omega_{0}=\tau,\quad\text{with}\quad\Im\tau>0

and
\phi_{\mathbf{q}}
be the normalized holomorphic
\mathbf{q}
-differential (the genus one case of formula (
5.10
))

\phi_{\mathbf{q}}=\frac{\omega_{0}(P)}{1+\tau\mathbf{q}},\quad\quad\text{with}\quad\quad\tau_{\mathbf{q}}:=\oint_{b}\phi_{\mathbf{q}}=\frac{\tau}{1+\tau\mathbf{q}}.

(5.46)

Here
\mathbf{q}
is a complex number such that
1+\tau\mathbf{q}\neq 0
.
By using Proposition
, we deduce that the system of flat coordinates (
5.25
) of the flat metric
\eta(\phi_{\mathbf{q}})
is given by

\begin{split}&t_{\mathbf{q},0}=\frac{1}{2{\rm i}\pi}\oint_{b}\phi_{\mathbf{q}}=\frac{\tau_{\mathbf{q}}}{2{\rm i}\pi};\\
&t_{\mathbf{q},k}:=s^{k}(\phi_{\mathbf{q}})=\int_{\infty^{0}}^{\infty^{k}}\phi_{\mathbf{q}}=\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{q}}(\infty^{k})-\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{q}}(\infty^{0}),\quad\quad k=1,\dots,m;\\
&t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-k}:=v^{k}(\phi_{\mathbf{q}})=\underset{\infty^{k}}{{\rm res}}\ \lambda(P)\phi_{\mathbf{q}}(P)=\phi_{\mathbf{q}}(\infty^{k}),\quad k=1,\dots,m;\\
&t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1}:=\xi(\phi_{\mathbf{q}})=\oint_{a}\lambda(P)\phi_{\mathbf{q}}(P)+\mathbf{q}\oint_{b}\lambda(P)\phi_{\mathbf{q}}(P).\end{split}

(5.47)

Therefore, by substituting this and
g=1
into (
5.45
) we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}_{\phi_{\mathbf{q}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j,k=1}^{m}t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-j}t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-k}s^{j}(\phi_{\mathbf{q},s^{k}})+t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1}\sum_{k=1}^{m}t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-k}t_{\mathbf{q},k}+\frac{(t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1})^{2}}{2}t_{\mathbf{q},0}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\log(-1)}{4}\sum_{j,k=1,j\neq k}^{m}t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-j}t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-k}-\frac{3}{4}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\big(1+\delta_{ij}\big)t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-j}t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-k}.

It remains to express functions
s^{j}(\phi_{\mathbf{q},s^{i}})
in terms of the flat coordinates (
5.47
). Making use of the link (
4.40
) between the theta function
\Theta_{[\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]}
with characteristics
[\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]
and the
\theta_{1}
-Jacobi odd function, the equalities (
5.47
) we can see that the genus one case of the functions (
5.40
) and (
5.41
) are respectively given by

\displaystyle s^{j}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},s^{k}}\big)

\displaystyle=\log\left(\frac{1}{1-{2{\rm i}\pi}\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\sum_{r=1}^{m}t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-r}\right)-\frac{2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}}{1-{2{\rm i}\pi}\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}t_{\mathbf{q},j}t_{\mathbf{q},k}

\displaystyle\quad+\log\left(\frac{\theta_{1}^{\prime}\left(0\Big|\frac{{2{\rm i}\pi}}{1-{2{\rm i}\pi}\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}t_{\mathbf{q},0}\right)\theta_{1}\left(\frac{t_{\mathbf{q},j}-t_{\mathbf{q},k}}{1-{2{\rm i}\pi}\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\Big|\frac{{2{\rm i}\pi}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}{1-{2{\rm i}\pi}\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\right)}{\theta_{1}\left(\frac{t_{\mathbf{q},j}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\Big|\frac{{2{\rm i}\pi}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}{1-{2{\rm i}\pi}\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\right)\theta_{1}\left(\frac{t_{\mathbf{q},k}}{1-{2{\rm i}\pi}\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\Big|\frac{{2{\rm i}\pi}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}{1-{2{\rm i}\pi}\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\right)}\right),\quad\quad\quad j\neq k

and

\displaystyle s^{k}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{q},s^{k}}\big)

\displaystyle=\log\left(\frac{t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-k}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\right)+\log\left(\frac{1}{1-{2{\rm i}\pi}\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\sum_{r=1}^{m}t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-r}\right)-\frac{2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\big(t_{\mathbf{q},k}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-2\log\left(\frac{\theta_{1}\left(\frac{t_{\mathbf{q},k}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\Big|\frac{{2{\rm i}\pi}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}{1-{2{\rm i}\pi}\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\right)}{\theta_{1}^{\prime}\left(0\Big|\frac{{2{\rm i}\pi}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}{1-{2{\rm i}\pi}\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\right)}\right).

Summarizing, we arrive at the following result.

Theorem 5.2

Consider the genus one Hurwitz space
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{1,m+1}(0,\dots,0)
of ramified coverings having
m+1
simple poles and
(2m+2)
-simple branch points.

Then Frobenius manifold structure on
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{1,m+1}(0,\dots,0)
induced by the normalized holomorphic
\mathbf{q}
-differential
\phi_{\mathbf{q}}
(
5.46
) is determined by the unit vector field
e=\partial_{t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1}}
, the Euler vector field
E=\sum_{k=0}^{m}t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-k}\partial_{t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-k}}

and the prepotential

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}_{\phi_{\mathbf{q}}}=\frac{(t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1})^{2}}{2}t_{\mathbf{q},0}+t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1}\sum_{k=1}^{m}t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-k}t_{\mathbf{q},k}-\frac{{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{m}t_{\mathbf{q},k}t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-k}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\big(t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-k}\big)^{2}\log\left(\frac{t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-k}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{m}t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-k}\right)^{2}\log\left(\sum_{r=1}^{m}\frac{t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-r}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{k=1}^{m}\big(t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-k}\big)^{2}\log\left(\frac{\theta_{1}\left(\frac{t_{\mathbf{q},k}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\Big|\frac{{2{\rm i}\pi}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\right)}{\theta_{1}^{\prime}\left(0\Big|\frac{{2{\rm i}\pi}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j,k=1,j\neq k}^{m}t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-j}t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-k}\log\left(\frac{\theta_{1}^{\prime}\left(0\Big|\frac{{2{\rm i}\pi}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\right)\theta_{1}\left(\frac{t_{\mathbf{q},j}-t_{\mathbf{q},k}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\Big|\frac{{2{\rm i}\pi}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\right)}{\theta_{1}\left(\frac{t_{\mathbf{q},j}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\Big|\frac{{2{\rm i}\pi}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\right)\theta_{1}\left(\frac{t_{\mathbf{q},k}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\Big|\frac{{2{\rm i}\pi}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\log(-1)}{4}\sum_{j,k=1,j\neq k}^{m}t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-j}t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-k}-\frac{3}{4}\sum_{j,k=1}^{m}\big(1+\delta_{jk}\big)t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-j}t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1-k}.

Let us mention the following remarks: Let
\phi_{\mathbf{q}}
be the holomorphic
\mathbf{q}
-differential defined by (
5.46
) and
\omega_{0}
be the normalized holomorphic differential i.e.
\oint_{a}\omega_{0}=1
.

•

Since by (
5.24
), for each
j=0,\dots,2m+1
, the
j
th flat coordinate
t_{\mathbf{q},j}
in (
5.47
) coincides with the function
t_{j}
when
\mathbf{q}=0
, where
t_{0},\dots,t_{2m+1}
are the functions defined by (
4.41
), we have

\mathbf{F}_{\phi_{\mathbf{q}}}\big(t_{\mathbf{q},0},\dots,t_{\mathbf{q},2m+1}\big)\Big|_{\mathbf{q}=0}=\mathbf{F}_{\omega_{0}}(t_{0},\dots,t_{2m+1})=\text{the prepotential given by (\ref {F-holo-g1})}.

•

When
m=1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}_{\phi_{\mathbf{q}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(t_{\mathbf{q},3})^{2}}{2}t_{\mathbf{q},0}+t_{\mathbf{q},1}t_{\mathbf{q},2}t_{\mathbf{q},3}-\frac{{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\left(t_{\mathbf{q},1}t_{\mathbf{q},2}\right)^{2}+\big(t_{\mathbf{q},2}\big)^{2}\log\left(\frac{t_{\mathbf{q},2}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\big(t_{\mathbf{q},2}\big)^{2}\log\left(\frac{\theta_{1}\left(\frac{t_{\mathbf{q},1}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\Big|\frac{{2{\rm i}\pi}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\right)}{\theta_{1}^{\prime}\left(0\Big|\frac{{2{\rm i}\pi}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}{1-2{\rm i}\pi\mathbf{q}t_{\mathbf{q},0}}\right)}\right)-\frac{3}{2}(t_{\mathbf{q},2})^{2}.

Up to a change of variables and the (unimportant) term
-\frac{3}{2}(t_{\mathbf{q},2})^{2}
, this example of prepotential has been computed in
[
11
]
.

6
Examples of solutions to the WDVV equations in genus zero

In this section, in Theorems
6.1
-
6.3
, we obtain explicit quasi-homogeneous solutions to the WDVV system depending on
2m
variables.

Consider the genus zero Hurwitz space of meromorphic functions
\lambda:\mathbb{P}^{1}\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}
given by

\lambda(P)=\sum_{k=0}^{m}\frac{b_{k}}{z_{P}-a_{k}}=\frac{b_{0}}{z_{P}}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{b_{k}}{z_{P}-a_{k}}

(6.1)

modulo automorphisms of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, where
m\geq 2
,
a_{0}=0,a_{1},\dots,a_{m}
are distinct points in
\mathbb{C}
and
b_{0},\dots,b_{m}
are nonzero complex numbers satisfying
b_{0}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{m}=1
. In particular
\lambda
has exactly
m+1
simple poles at
a_{0},\dots,a_{m}
and the point at infinity
\infty
is one of its zeros.

We have

\lambda^{\prime}(P)=-\frac{f_{2m}(z_{P})}{\prod_{k=0}^{m}(z_{P}-a_{k})^{2}},\quad\text{with}\quad f_{2m}(z_{P})=\sum_{k=0}^{m}b_{k}\prod_{j=0,j\neq k}^{m}\big(z_{P}-a_{j}\big)^{2}.

We observe that the assumption
b_{0}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{m}=1
implies that the leading coefficient of the
2m
-degree polynomial
f_{2m}
is 1. We also assume that
f_{2m}
has
2m
distinct roots, denoted by
\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{2m}
. Let
P_{j}\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
be the point represented by the complex number
z_{P_{j}}=\alpha_{j}
and
\lambda_{j}=\lambda(P_{j})
be its image by
\lambda
. Thus
P_{1},\dots,P_{2m}
(resp.
\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{2m}
) are nothing but the ramification points (resp. the branch points) of the branched covering
(\mathbb{P}^{1},\lambda)
.

Since
a_{0}=0
and the complex numbers
b_{0},a_{1},\dots,a_{m}
are all nonzero (by assumption), we have

f_{2m}(0)=\sum_{k=0}^{m}b_{k}\prod_{j=0,j\neq k}^{m}a_{j}^{2}=b_{0}\prod_{j=1}^{m}a_{j}^{2}\neq 0.

In particular, none of the ramification points
\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{2m}
is zero.

Following the notation introduced in Section 2.2, the simple Hurwitz space consisting of meromorphic functions
\lambda
(
6.1
), modulo automorphisms of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, is denoted by
\mathcal{H}_{0,m+1}(0,\dots,0)
.
This space can be identified with the open subset of points
(a_{1},\dots,a_{m},b_{1},\dots,b_{m})\in\big(\mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\}\big)^{2m}
determined by the conditions:

1)

\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{2m}
are distinct in
\mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\}
;

2)

the branch points
\{\lambda_{j}\}_{j}
satisfy
\lambda_{i}\neq\lambda_{j}
whenever
i\neq j
.

Let us now consider the family of Frobenius structures on the Hurwitz space
\mathcal{H}_{0,m+1}(0,\dots,0)
.
According to (
3.23
), we only have two types of Dubrovin’s primary differentials:

\begin{split}&\phi_{k}(P)=\int_{0}^{a_{k}}W(P,Q)=\frac{dz_{P}}{z_{P}-a_{k}}-\frac{dz_{P}}{z_{P}}=\Omega_{0a_{k}}(P);\\
&\phi_{2m+1-k}(P)=\underset{a_{k}}{{\rm res}}\lambda(Q)W(P,Q)=\frac{b_{k}}{(z_{P}-a_{k})^{2}}dz_{P},\end{split}\quad\quad\quad\forall\ k=1,\dots,m,

(6.2)

with
W(P,Q)
being the genus zero bidifferential (
2.14
).

As we know from Definition
4.2
and Proposition
4.11
, primary differentials in (
6.2
) are also quasi-homogeneous and their degrees are given by:

{\rm deg}(\phi_{k})=0\quad\quad\text{and}\quad\quad{\rm deg}(\phi_{2m+1-k})=1,\quad\quad\forall\ k=1,\dots,m.

(6.3)

We shall also study the Frobenius manifold structure associated with the following quasi-homogeneous differential
\phi_{0}
of degree 0:

\phi_{0}(P)=\int_{0}^{\infty}W(P,Q)=-\frac{dz_{P}}{z_{P}}.

(6.4)

Besides leading to an example of non-normalized Frobenius manifold structure (i.e. where the unity is flat), considering differential (
6.4
) turns out to be useful in calculating examples of prepotentials corresponding to the primary differentials (
6.2
).

Proposition 6.1

Let
\eta(\phi_{k}):=\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j}\big(\phi_{k}(P_{j})\big)^{2}(d\lambda_{j})^{2}
be the Darboux-Egoroff metrics (
3.20
) induced by the Abelian differential
\phi_{k}
,
k=0,1,\dots,2m
, given by (
6.2
) and (
6.4
).

Then

\displaystyle\phi_{0}(P_{j})^{2}=-2\frac{\prod_{r=1}^{m}(\alpha_{j}-a_{r})^{2}}{\prod_{s=1,s\neq j}^{2m}(\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{s})};

\displaystyle\phi_{k}(P_{j})^{2}=-2a_{k}^{2}\frac{\prod_{r=1,r\neq k}^{m}(\alpha_{j}-a_{r})^{2}}{\prod_{s=1,s\neq j}^{2m}(\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{s})},\hskip 71.13188ptk=1,\dots,m;

\displaystyle\phi_{2m+1-k}(P_{j})^{2}=-2b_{k}^{2}\frac{\alpha_{j}^{2}\prod_{r=1,r\neq k}^{m}(\alpha_{j}-a_{r})^{2}}{(\alpha_{j}-a_{k})^{2}\prod_{s=1,s\neq j}^{2m}(\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{s})},\quad\quad k=1,\dots,m.

Proof:
Due to (
2.6
), we have

\displaystyle\phi_{0}(P_{j})^{2}=2\underset{\alpha_{j}}{{\rm res}}\frac{\phi_{0}(P)^{2}}{d\lambda(P)}=2\underset{\alpha_{j}}{{\rm res}}\frac{dz_{P}}{z_{P}^{2}\lambda^{\prime}(P)};

\displaystyle\phi_{k}(P_{j})^{2}=2\underset{\alpha_{j}}{{\rm res}}\frac{\phi_{k}(P)^{2}}{d\lambda(P)}=2\underset{\alpha_{j}}{{\rm res}}\frac{a_{k}^{2}dz_{P}}{z_{P}^{2}(z_{P}-a_{k})^{2}\lambda^{\prime}(P)};

\displaystyle\phi_{2m+1-k}(P_{j})^{2}=2\underset{\alpha_{j}}{{\rm res}}\frac{b_{k}^{2}dz_{P}}{(z_{P}-a_{k})^{4}\lambda^{\prime}(P)}.

Thus by simple calculations using the fact that

\lambda^{\prime}(P)=-\frac{\prod_{s=1}^{2m}\big(z_{P}-\alpha_{s}\big)}{\prod_{s=0}^{m}(z_{P}-a_{s})^{2}},

we obtain the desired results.

\square

Let
k=0,\dots,2m
be fixed. Then the system of flat coordinates (
3.21
) of the metric
\eta(\phi_{k})
reduces to the following:

\begin{split}&\mathbf{t}^{s}(\phi_{k})=p.v.\int_{0}^{a_{s}}\phi_{k}(P);\\
&\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-s}(\phi_{k})=\underset{a_{s}}{{\rm res}}\lambda(P)\phi_{k}(P),\end{split}\quad\quad\quad\forall\ s=1,\dots,m.

(6.5)

Here we would like to mention that the ordering of the functions
\mathbf{t}^{j}
,
j=1,\dots,2m
, is chosen by respecting the correspondence (
3.13
) and (
3.14
)
between flat coordinates and primary differentials.

Furthermore, the
\eta(\phi_{k})
-duality between flat coordinates (introduced in (
3.40
) and (
3.41
)) is described by the relation

\mathbf{t}^{s^{\prime}}(\phi_{k})=\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-s}(\phi_{k}),\quad\forall\ s=1,\dots,2m.

(6.6)

and thus the metric
\eta(\phi_{k})
becomes as follows (see formula (
3.45
)):

\displaystyle\eta(\phi_{k})=\sum_{s=1}^{2m}d\mathbf{t}^{s}(\phi_{k})\otimes d\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-s}(\phi_{k}).

We first calculate flat coordinates of the Darboux-Egoroff metric
\eta(\phi_{0})
by means of the parameters
a_{1},\dots,a_{m},b_{0},b_{1},\dots,b_{m}
of the Hurwitz space
\mathcal{H}_{0,m+1}(0,\dots,0)
.

Proposition 6.2

Let
\phi_{0}
be the differential
\phi_{0}(P)=-dz_{P}/z_{P}
. Then, the flat coordinates (
6.5
) of the metric
\eta(\phi_{0})
are:

\begin{split}&t_{s}:=\mathbf{t}^{s}(\phi_{0})=p.v.\int_{0}^{a_{s}}\phi_{0}(P)=\log(b_{0})-\log(a_{s});\\
&t_{2m+1-s}:=\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-s}(\phi_{0})=\underset{a_{s}}{{\rm res}}\lambda(P)\phi_{0}(P)=-\frac{b_{s}}{a_{s}},\end{split}\quad\quad\quad\forall\ s=1,\dots,m.

(6.7)

Proof:
Let
P^{\varepsilon}
be the point of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
such that
z_{P^{\varepsilon}}=\varepsilon\in\mathbb{C}
where
|\varepsilon|
is small enough.
Since, by (
6.1
),

\varepsilon\lambda(P^{\varepsilon})=b_{0}+\varepsilon\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{b_{j}}{\varepsilon-a_{j}},

it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{\varepsilon}^{a_{s}}\phi_{0}(P)

\displaystyle=-\int_{\varepsilon}^{a_{s}}\frac{dz_{P}}{z_{P}}=\log(\varepsilon)-\log(a_{s})=\log\bigg(b_{0}+\varepsilon\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{b_{j}}{\varepsilon-a_{j}}\bigg)-\log\big(\lambda(P^{\varepsilon})\big)-\log(a_{s}).

Therefore, by definition of the principal value from Section 3.3, we obtain

\displaystyle t_{s}:=p.v.\int_{0}^{a_{s}}\phi_{0}(P)=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\bigg(\int_{\varepsilon}^{a_{s}}\phi_{0}(P)+\log\big(\lambda(P^{\varepsilon})\big)\bigg)=\log(b_{0})-\log(a_{s}).

On the other hand, by straightforward calculation we see that
t_{2m+1-s}:=\underset{a_{s}}{\rm res}\lambda(P)\phi_{0}(P)=-b_{s}/a_{s}
.

\square

The purpose of the next result is to find flat coordinates (
6.5
) corresponding to the primary differentials listed in (
6.2
). For later use, we express them by means of flat coordinates
t_{s},t_{2m+1-s}
(
6.7
) corresponding to the differential
\phi_{0}(P)=-dz_{P}/z_{P}
.

Proposition 6.3

Let
k=1,\dots,m
.

i)

Flat coordinates of the metric
\eta(\phi_{k})
are given in terms of the flat coordinates
t_{s},t_{2m+1-s}
from (
6.7
) by:

\begin{split}&x_{k,s}:=\mathbf{t}^{s}(\phi_{k})=\log\big(e^{t_{s}}-e^{t_{k}}\big),\quad\quad s\neq k;\\
&x_{k,k}:=\mathbf{t}^{k}(\phi_{k})=t_{k}+\log(t_{2m+1-k});\\
&x_{k,2m+1-s}:=\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-s}(\phi_{k})=t_{2m+1-s}\frac{e^{t_{s}}}{e^{t_{s}}-e^{t_{k}}},\quad\quad s\neq k;\\
&x_{k,2m+1-k}:=\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-k}(\phi_{k})=e^{t_{k}}+\sum_{s=1}^{m}t_{2m+1-s}-\sum_{s=1,s\neq k}^{m}x_{k,2m+1-s}.\end{split}

(6.8)

ii)

Flat coordinates of
\eta(\phi_{2m+1-k})
are:

\begin{split}&y_{k,s}:=\mathbf{t}^{s}(\phi_{2m+1-k})=t_{2m+1-k}\frac{e^{t_{k}}}{e^{t_{k}}-e^{t_{s}}}=x_{s,2m+1-k},\quad\quad s\neq k;\\
&y_{k,k}:=\mathbf{t}^{k}(\phi_{2m+1-k})=e^{t_{k}}+\sum_{s=1}^{m}t_{2m+1-s}-\sum_{s=1,s\neq k}^{m}y_{s,k}=x_{k,2m+1-k};\\
&y_{k,2m+1-s}:=\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-s}(\phi_{2m+1-k})=\frac{t_{2m+1-k}t_{2m+1-s}e^{t_{k}+t_{s}}}{\big(e^{t_{k}}-e^{t_{s}}\big)^{2}},\quad\quad s\neq k;\\
&y_{k,2m+1-k}:=\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-k}(\phi_{2m+1-k})=t_{2m+1-k}e^{t_{k}}-\sum_{s=1,s\neq k}^{m}y_{k,2m+1-s}.\end{split}

(6.9)

Proof:
Let us first point out that, due to (
6.7
), the following relations are satisfied:

\begin{split}&a_{j}=b_{0}e^{-t_{j}};\\
&b_{j}=-a_{j}t_{2m+1-j}=-b_{0}t_{2m+1-j}e^{-t_{j}},\end{split}\quad\quad\forall\ j=1,\dots,m.

(6.10)

Let
P^{\varepsilon},P^{\delta}\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
be such that
z_{P^{\varepsilon}}=\varepsilon
and
z_{P^{\delta}}=a_{k}+\delta
, where
\varepsilon,\delta\in\mathbb{C}
are
such that
|\varepsilon|
and
|\delta|
are sufficiently small.

i)
Assume that
s\neq k
. We have

\displaystyle\int_{\varepsilon}^{a_{s}}\phi_{k}(P)

\displaystyle=\log(a_{s}-a_{k})-\log(\varepsilon-a_{k})-\log(a_{s})+\log(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\textstyle\log(a_{s}-a_{k})-\log(\varepsilon-a_{k})-\log(a_{s})-\log\big(\lambda(P^{\varepsilon})\big)+\log\Big(b_{0}+\varepsilon\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{b_{j}}{-a_{j}+\varepsilon}\Big).

Therefore, using this and (
6.10
) we arrive at

\displaystyle x_{k,s}

\displaystyle:=p.v.\int_{0}^{a_{s}}\phi_{k}(P)=\log(a_{k}-a_{s})-\log(a_{k})-\log(a_{s})+\log(b_{0})

\displaystyle=\log\big(1-(a_{s}/a_{k})\big)-\log(a_{s})+\log(b_{0})

\displaystyle=\log\big(e^{t_{s}}-e^{t_{k}}\big).

In the same way we get

\displaystyle x_{k,k}=\log(b_{k})+\log(b_{0})-2\log(a_{k})-\log(-1)=t_{k}+\log(t_{2m+1-k}).

On the other hand, (
6.10
) implies that

\displaystyle\forall\ s\neq k,\quad x_{k,2m+1-s}

\displaystyle:=\underset{a_{s}}{{\rm res}}\lambda(P)\phi_{k}(P)=\frac{b_{s}}{a_{s}-a_{k}}-\frac{b_{s}}{a_{s}}

\displaystyle=t_{2m+1-s}-\frac{t_{2m+1-s}e^{-t_{s}}}{e^{-t_{s}}-e^{-t_{k}}}

\displaystyle=t_{2m+1-s}\frac{e^{t_{s}}}{e^{t_{s}}-e^{t_{k}}}.

Furthermore, in view of the residue theorem and (
6.10
), we obtain

\displaystyle x_{k,2m+1-k}

\displaystyle:=\underset{a_{k}}{{\rm res}}\lambda(P)\phi_{k}(P)=-\underset{0}{{\rm res}}\lambda(P)\phi_{k}(P)-\sum_{s=1,s\neq k}^{m}\underset{a_{s}}{{\rm res}}\lambda(P)\phi_{k}(P)

\displaystyle=\frac{b_{0}}{a_{k}}-\sum_{s=1}^{m}\frac{b_{s}}{a_{s}}-\sum_{s=1,s\neq k}^{m}x_{k,2m+1-s}

\displaystyle=e^{t_{k}}+\sum_{s=1}^{m}t_{2m+1-s}-\sum_{s=1,s\neq k}^{m}x_{k,2m+1-s}.

ii)
When
s\neq k
, then

\displaystyle y_{k,s}

\displaystyle:=p.v.\int_{0}^{a_{k}}\phi_{2m+1-k}(P)=p.v.\int_{0}^{a_{s}}\frac{b_{k}}{(z_{P}-a_{k})^{2}}dz_{P}=\int_{0}^{a_{s}}\frac{b_{k}}{(z_{P}-a_{k})^{2}}dz_{P}

\displaystyle=\frac{b_{k}}{a_{k}-a_{s}}-\frac{b_{k}}{a_{k}}=x_{s,2m+1-k}.

If
s=k
, then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{a_{k}+\delta}\frac{b_{k}}{(z_{P}-a_{k})^{2}}dz_{P}

\displaystyle=-\frac{b_{k}}{\delta}-\frac{b_{k}}{a_{k}}=-\frac{b_{k}}{a_{k}}+\sum_{j=0,j\neq k}^{m}\frac{b_{j}}{a_{k}-a_{j}+\delta}-\lambda(P^{\delta}).

By subtracting the divergent term
\lambda(P^{\delta})
, letting
\delta\to 0
and employing (
6.10
), we get

\displaystyle y_{k,k}

\displaystyle:=p.v.\int_{0}^{a_{k}}\phi_{2m+1-k}(P)=-\frac{b_{k}}{a_{k}}+\sum_{j=0,j\neq k}^{m}\frac{b_{j}}{a_{k}-a_{j}}

\displaystyle=\frac{b_{0}}{a_{k}}-\sum_{s=1}^{m}\frac{b_{k}}{a_{k}}-\sum_{s=1,s\neq k}\bigg(\frac{b_{s}}{a_{s}-a_{k}}-\frac{b_{s}}{a_{s}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=e^{t_{k}}+\sum_{s=1}^{m}t_{2m+1-s}-\sum_{s=1,s\neq k}^{m}x_{k,2m+1-s}=x_{k,2m+1-k}.

Finally, let us deal with the functions
y_{k,2m+1-s}=\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-s}\big(\phi_{2m+1-k}\big)
, with
s=1,\dots,m
.
Due to (
6.10
) we have

\displaystyle\forall\ s\neq k,\quad y_{k,2m+1-s}

\displaystyle:=\underset{a_{s}}{{\rm res}}\lambda(P)\phi_{2m+1-k}(P)=\frac{b_{s}b_{k}}{(a_{s}-a_{k})^{2}}=\frac{t_{2m+1-k}t_{2m+1-s}e^{t_{k}+t_{s}}}{\big(e^{t_{k}}-e^{t_{s}}\big)^{2}}.

In addition, the residue theorem applied to the Abelian differential
\lambda(P)\phi_{2m+1-k}(P)
yields

\displaystyle y_{k,2m+1-k}

\displaystyle:=\underset{a_{k}}{{\rm res}}\lambda(P)\phi_{2m+1-k}(P)=-\underset{0}{{\rm res}}\lambda(P)\phi_{2m+1-k}(P)-\sum_{s=1,s\neq k}^{m}\underset{a_{s}}{{\rm res}}\lambda(P)\phi_{2m+1-k}(P)

\displaystyle=-\frac{b_{0}b_{k}}{a_{k}^{2}}-\sum_{s=1,s\neq k}y_{k,2m+1-s}=t_{2m+1-k}e^{t_{k}}-\sum_{s=1,s\neq k}y_{k,2m+1-s}.

\square

The following corollary follows directly from (
6.8
) and (
6.9
).

Corollary 6.1

For any fixed
k=1,\dots,m
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{s=1}^{m}x_{k,2m+1-s}=e^{t_{k}}+\sum_{s=1}^{m}t_{2m+1-s}=\sum_{s=1}^{m}y_{s,k};\end{split}

(6.11)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{s=1}^{m}y_{k,2m+1-s}=t_{2m+1-k}e^{t_{k}}=e^{x_{k,k}}.\end{split}

(6.12)

Since the branch points
\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{2m}
play the role of canonical coordinates of the semi-simple Frobenius manifolds associated with the Abelian differentials
\phi_{0},\phi_{1},\dots,\phi_{2m}
, it follows that the unit and Euler vector fields are respectively given by

\mathbf{1}=\sum_{j=1}^{2m}\partial_{\lambda_{j}}\quad\quad\text{and}\quad\quad\mathbf{E}=\sum_{j=1}^{2m}\lambda_{j}\partial_{\lambda_{j}}.

(6.13)

We are going to give the expressions for these vector fields in each system of flat coordinates attached to the considered differentials
\phi_{k}
,
k=0,\dots,2m
.

Proposition 6.4

For
j=0,1,\dots,2m
, denote by
\mathbf{1}_{\phi_{j}}
(resp.
\mathbf{E}_{\phi_{j}}
) the vector field
\mathbf{1}
(resp.
\mathbf{E}
) (
6.13
) written in the flat coordinates (
6.5
) of the metric
\eta(\phi_{j})
.

i)

Let
k=1,\dots,m
. In flat coordinates (
6.8
), we have

\begin{split}&\mathbf{1}_{\phi_{k}}=\partial_{x_{k,2m+1-k}};\\
&\mathbf{E}_{\phi_{k}}=\sum_{s=1}^{m}\bigg((1+\delta_{ks})\partial_{x_{k,s}}+x_{k,2m+1-s}\partial_{x_{k,2m+1-s}}\bigg).\end{split}

(6.14)

ii)

Let
k=1,\dots,m
. Then in flat coordinates (
6.9
), we have

\begin{split}&\mathbf{1}_{\phi_{2m+1-k}}=\partial_{y_{k,k}};\\
&\mathbf{E}_{\phi_{2m+1-k}}=\sum_{s=1}^{m}\bigg(y_{k,s}\partial_{y_{k,s}}+2y_{k,2m+1-s}\partial_{y_{k,2m+1-s}}\bigg).\end{split}

(6.15)

iii)

In flat coordinates (
6.7
), the vector fields (
6.13
) have respectively the following forms:

\begin{split}&\mathbf{1}_{\phi_{0}}=\frac{1}{1-\sum_{r=1}^{m}t_{2m+1-r}e^{-t_{r}}}\sum_{s=1}^{m}e^{-t_{s}}\Big(\partial_{t_{s}}-t_{2m+1-s}\partial_{t_{2m+1-s}}\Big);\\
&\mathbf{E}_{\phi_{0}}=\sum_{s=1}^{m}\Big(\partial_{t_{s}}+t_{2m+1-s}\partial_{t_{2m+1-s}}\Big).\end{split}

(6.16)

Note that (
6.7
) and the assumption
1=b_{0}+\dots+b_{m}
imply that

1-\sum_{r=1}^{m}t_{2m+1-r}e^{-t_{r}}=1+\frac{1}{b_{0}}\sum_{r=1}^{m}b_{r}=\frac{1}{b_{0}}\neq 0.

Proof:

i)
and
ii)
Given that
\phi_{k}
and
\phi_{2m+1-k}
(
6.2
), with
k=1,\dots,m
, are primary differentials, using formula (
4.9
), duality relations (
6.6
) and the systems (
6.8
) and (
6.9
) of flat coordinates, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\phi_{k}}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{t}^{k^{\prime}}(\phi_{k})}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-k}(\phi_{k})}=\partial_{x_{k,2m+1-k}};

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\phi_{2m+1-k}}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{t}^{(2m+1-k)^{\prime}}\big(\phi_{2m+1-k}\big)}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{t}^{k}\big(\phi_{2m+1-k}\big)}=\partial_{y_{k,k}}.

Furthermore, formula (
4.17
) reads in our setting as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{\phi_{j}}=\sum_{s=1}^{2m}\Big((d_{j}+d_{s})\mathbf{t}^{s}(\phi_{j})+r_{j,s}\Big)\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{t}^{s}(\phi_{j})},\quad\forall\ j=1,\dots,2m,

where
d_{j}={\rm deg}(\phi_{j})
and the constants
r_{j,k}
are the analogous of (
4.16
) (see also (
3.57
)) and given by

r_{j,s}=\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}=1}^{m}\delta_{\phi_{j},\phi_{l_{1}}}\delta_{\phi_{s},\phi_{l_{2}}}\big(1+\delta_{l_{1}l_{2}}),\quad\quad\forall\ j,s=1,\dots,2m.

Here we used the fact the poles of the considered meromorphic functions (
6.1
) are all simple.

This and (
6.3
) as well as notation (
6.8
) imply that

\mathbf{E}_{\phi_{k}}=\sum_{s=1}^{m}\bigg((1+\delta_{ks})\partial_{x_{k,s}}+x_{k,2m+1-s}\partial_{x_{k,2m+1-s}}\bigg),\quad\forall\ k=1,\dots,m.

By the same arguments, we also obtain the desired formula for
\mathbf{E}_{\phi_{2m+1-k}}
,
k=1,\dots,m
.

iii)
In view of (
6.8
), we have

\displaystyle x_{k,s}=\log\big(e^{t_{s}}-e^{t_{k}}\big)=x_{s,k}+\log(-1),\quad\quad s\neq k;

\displaystyle x_{k,k}=t_{k}+\log(t_{2m+1-k});

\displaystyle x_{k,2m+1-s}=t_{2m+1-s}e^{t_{s}-x_{k,s}},\quad\quad s\neq k;

\displaystyle x_{k,2m+1-k}=e^{t_{k}}+\sum_{s=1}^{m}t_{2m+1-s}-\sum_{s=1,s\neq k}^{m}x_{k,2m+1-s}.

Furthermore, using the fact that
\mathbf{1}=\mathbf{1}_{\phi_{k}}=\partial_{x_{k,2m+1-k}}
we deduce that the action of unit vector field
\mathbf{1}=\sum_{j=1}^{2m}\partial_{\lambda_{j}}
on the coordinates
t_{s}
,
t_{2m+1-s}
,
x_{k,s}
,
x_{k,2m+1-s}
(regarded as functions of branch points

\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{2m}
) leads to the following equations:

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}\big(x_{k,s}\big)=0=\mathbf{1}(t_{s})e^{t_{s}}-\mathbf{1}(t_{k})e^{t_{k}},\quad\quad s\neq k;

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}\big(x_{k,k}\big)=0=\mathbf{1}(t_{k})+\frac{\mathbf{1}\big(t_{2m+1-k}\big)}{t_{2m+1-k}};

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}\big(x_{k,2m+1-s}\big)=0=\mathbf{1}\big(t_{2m+1-s}\big)e^{t_{s}-x_{k,s}}+\mathbf{1}(t_{s})t_{2m+1-s}e^{t_{s}-x_{k,s}},\quad\quad s\neq k;

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}\big(x_{k,2m+1-k}\big)=1=\mathbf{1}(t_{k})e^{t_{k}}+\sum_{s=1}^{m}\mathbf{1}\big(t_{2m+1-s}\big).

From this we arrive at

\begin{split}&\bigg(1-\sum_{r=1}^{m}t_{2m+1-r}e^{-t_{r}}\bigg)\mathbf{1}(t_{s})=e^{-t_{s}};\\
&\bigg(1-\sum_{r=1}^{m}t_{2m+1-r}e^{-t_{r}}\bigg)\mathbf{1}(t_{2m+1-s})=-t_{2m+1-s}e^{-t_{s}},\end{split}\quad\quad\forall\ s=1,\dots,m.

This shows the first stated relation in (
6.16
).

Alternatively, the expression for
\mathbf{1}_{\phi_{0}}
can be proven using the following facts (this method was indicated at the beginning of Section 4.3):

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}\big(\mathbf{t}^{k}\big(\phi_{0})\big)=\sum_{j=1}^{2m}\underset{P_{j}}{\rm res}\ \frac{\phi_{0}(P)\phi_{k}(P)}{d\lambda(P)}=-\phi_{k}(\infty)=-\underset{\infty}{\rm res}\ \frac{\phi_{k}(P)}{\lambda(P)}=a_{k};

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}\big(\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-k}\big(\phi_{0})\big)=\sum_{j=1}^{2m}\underset{P_{j}}{\rm res}\ \frac{\phi_{0}(P)\phi_{2m+1-k}(P)}{d\lambda(P)}=-\phi_{2m+1-k}(\infty)=-\underset{\infty}{\rm res}\ \frac{\phi_{2m+1-k}(P)}{\lambda(P)}=b_{k},

where we have used the residue theorem, relation (
2.7
) and expressions (
6.1
) and (
6.2
) for
\lambda
and differentials
\phi_{k}
,
\phi_{2m+1-k}
. Hence, in view of (
6.10
), we get the desired relation.

On the other hand, we know that
\phi_{0}
is the Abelian differential of the third kind
\phi_{0}=\Omega_{0\infty}
(here the point at infinity
\infty
is a zero of
\lambda
and
0
is a simple pole of
\lambda
) and quasi-homogeneous of degree 0. Thus, applying (
4.17
) we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{\phi_{0}}=\sum_{s=1}^{2m}\Big(d_{s}\mathbf{t}^{s}(\phi_{0})+r_{\phi_{0},\phi_{s}}\Big)\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{t}^{s}(\phi_{0})}=\sum_{s=1}^{m}\Big(\partial_{t_{s}}+t_{2m+1-s}\partial_{t_{2m+1-s}}\Big).

\square

Let us now move on to calculate explicit solutions to the WDVV equations that are associated with the described
\phi_{j}
-semi-simple Frobenius manifold structures,
j=0,\dots,2m
.

In our setting, the generalized
\phi_{j}
-WDVV equations (
1.6
) take the following form:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\big(\partial_{\mathbf{t}^{a}(\phi_{j})}\mathbf{H}_{\phi_{j}}\big)\eta\big(\partial_{\mathbf{t}^{b}(\phi_{j})}\mathbf{H}_{\phi_{j}}\big)=\big(\partial_{\mathbf{t}^{b}(\phi_{j})}\mathbf{H}_{\phi_{j}}\big)\eta\big(\partial_{\mathbf{t}^{a}(\phi_{j})}\mathbf{H}_{\phi_{j}}\big),&\hbox{}\quad a,b=1,\dots,2m;\\
\\
\eta=\sum_{a=1}^{2m}\mathbf{1}_{\phi_{j}}\big(\mathbf{t}^{a}(\phi_{j})\big)\big(\partial_{\mathbf{t}^{a}(\phi_{j})}\mathbf{H}_{\phi_{j}}\big).\end{array}\right.

Here
\mathbf{H}_{\phi_{j}}
is the Hessian matrix of the prepotential
\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{j}}
induced by
\phi_{j}
and
the
2m\times 2m
constant matrix
\eta
is anti-diagonal with
\eta_{ab}=\delta_{2m+1,a+b}=\eta^{ab}
.

Note that according to (
6.14
), (
6.15
) and (
6.16
), the second line of the preceding equations reads as:

\eta=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\displaystyle\partial_{x_{k,2m+1-k}}\mathbf{H}_{\phi_{k}},&\hbox{if}\quad k=1,\dots,m;\\
\\
\displaystyle\partial_{y_{k,k}}\mathbf{H}_{\phi_{2m+1-k}},&\hbox{if}\quad k=1,\dots,m;\\
\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\sum_{r=1}^{m}t_{2m+1-r}e^{-t_{r}}}\sum_{s=1}^{m}e^{-t_{s}}\Big(\partial_{t_{s}}\mathbf{H}_{\phi_{0}}-t_{2m+1-s}\partial_{t_{2m+1-s}}\mathbf{H}_{\phi_{0}}\Big),&\hbox{if}\quad k=0.\end{array}\right.

For our purpose, we calculate the
\phi_{j}
-WDVV solutions
\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{j}}
applying formula (
4.29
) (we choose to omit the quadratic terms to simplify formulas).

Employing notation (
6.5
), formula (
4.29
) tells us that the prepotential associated with the differential
\phi_{j}
is as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{j}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2(1+d_{j})}\sum_{k,s=1}^{2m}\frac{\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{k}(\phi_{j})\big)\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{s}(\phi_{j})\big)}{1+d_{j}+{\rm deg}(\phi_{2m+1-k})}\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-s}\big(\phi_{2m+1-k}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2(1+d_{j})(2+d_{j})}\sum_{k,s=1}^{m}\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{k}(\phi_{j})\big)\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{s}(\phi_{j})\big)\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-s}\big(\phi_{2m+1-k}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2(1+d_{j})(2+d_{j})}\sum_{k,s=1}^{m}\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{k}(\phi_{j})\big)\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-s}(\phi_{j})\big)\mathbf{t}^{s}\big(\phi_{2m+1-k}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2(1+d_{j})^{2}}\sum_{k,s=1}^{m}\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-k}(\phi_{j})\big)\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{s}(\phi_{j})\big)\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-s}\big(\phi_{k}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2(1+d_{j})^{2}}\sum_{k,s=1}^{m}\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-k}(\phi_{j})\big)\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-s}(\phi_{j})\big)\mathbf{t}^{s}\big(\phi_{k}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2(1+d_{j})(2+d_{j})}\sum_{k,s=1}^{m}\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{k}(\phi_{j})\big)\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{s}(\phi_{j})\big)y_{k,2m+1-s}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2d_{j}+3}{2(1+d_{j})^{2}(2+d_{j})}\sum_{k,s=1}^{m}\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{s}(\phi_{j})\big)\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-k}(\phi_{j})\big)x_{k,2m+1-s}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2(1+d_{j})^{2}}\sum_{k,s=1}^{m}\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-k}(\phi_{j})\big)\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-s}(\phi_{j})\big)x_{k,s},

where we used duality relations (
6.6
) in the first equality, relation (
6.3
) giving the degree of
\phi_{k}
in the second equality.
Moreover, the last equality follows from the introduced notation in (
6.8
) and (
6.9
) as well as the relation

y_{k,s}:=\mathbf{t}^{s}\big(\phi_{2m+1-k}\big)=\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-s}\big(\phi_{k}\big):=x_{s,2m+1-k}.

Therefore, we arrive at

\begin{split}\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{j}}&=\frac{1}{2(1+d_{j})(2+d_{j})}\sum_{k,s=1}^{m}\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{k}(\phi_{j})\big)\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{s}(\phi_{j})\big)y_{k,2m+1-s}\\
&\quad+\frac{2d_{j}+3}{2(1+d_{j})^{2}(2+d_{j})}\sum_{k,s=1}^{m}\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{s}(\phi_{j})\big)\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-k}(\phi_{j})\big)x_{k,2m+1-s}\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{2(1+d_{j})^{2}}\sum_{k,s=1}^{m}\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-k}(\phi_{j})\big)\big(E.\mathbf{t}^{2m+1-s}(\phi_{j})\big)x_{k,s}.\end{split}

(6.17)

In the next Theorems, we write (
6.17
) for various concrete differentials
phi_{j}
, thus obtaining explicit prepotentials of the corresponding Frobenius manifolds.

Theorem 6.1

Let
\phi_{0}
be the differential (
6.4
). The
\phi_{0}
-prepotential is:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{0}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{m}t_{2m+1-k}e^{t_{k}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\big(t_{2m+1-k}\big)^{2}\Big(t_{k}+\log\big(t_{2m+1-k}\big)\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k,s=1,s\neq k}^{m}t_{2m+1-k}t_{2m+1-s}\log\big(e^{t_{s}}-e^{t_{k}}\big).

The function
\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{0}}
is quasi-homogeneous of degree 2 with respect to the Euler vector field
\mathbf{E}_{\phi_{0}}
(
6.16
) and we have

\mathbf{E}_{\phi_{0}}.\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{0}}=2\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{0}}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\big(t_{2m+1-k}\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k,s=1,s\neq k}^{m}t_{2m+1-k}t_{2m+1-s}.

Moreover,
\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{0}}
provides a solution to the generalized WDVV equations (
1.6
).

Proof:
We have
{\rm deg}(\phi_{0})=0
and by (
6.16
), the following quasi-homogeneity property of the flat coordinates
t_{k},t_{2m+1-k}
(
6.7
) are satisfied:

E.t_{s}=1,\quad E.t_{2m+1-s}=t_{2m+1-s},\quad\forall\ s=1,\dots,m.

This and formula (
6.17
) imply that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{0}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k,s=1}^{m}y_{k,2m+1-s}+\frac{3}{4}\sum_{k,s=1}^{m}t_{2m+1-k}x_{k,2m+1-s}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k,s=1}^{m}t_{2m+1-k}t_{2m+1-s}x_{k,s}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\bigg(\sum_{s=1}^{m}y_{k,2m+1-s}\bigg)+\frac{3}{4}\sum_{k=1}^{m}t_{2m+1-k}\bigg(\sum_{s=1}^{m}x_{k,2m+1-s}\bigg)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\big(t_{2m+1-k}\big)^{2}x_{k,k}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k,s=1,s\neq k}^{m}t_{2m+1-k}t_{2m+1-s}x_{k,s}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k=1}^{m}t_{2m+1-k}e^{t_{k}}+\frac{3}{4}\sum_{k=1}^{m}t_{2m+1-k}\bigg(e^{t_{k}}+\sum_{s=1}^{m}t_{2m+1-s}\bigg)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\big(t_{2m+1-k}\big)^{2}\Big(t_{k}+\log\big(t_{2m+1-k}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k,s=1,s\neq k}^{m}t_{2m+1-k}t_{2m+1-s}\log\big(e^{t_{s}}-e^{t_{k}}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{m}t_{2m+1-k}e^{t_{k}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\big(t_{2m+1-k}\big)^{2}\Big(t_{k}+\log\big(t_{2m+1-k}\big)\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k,s=1,s\neq k}^{m}t_{2m+1-k}t_{2m+1-s}\log\big(e^{t_{s}}-e^{t_{k}}\big).

Here, in the third equality, we used (
6.11
) and (
6.12
) and replaced functions
x_{k,s}
(
6.8
),
s=1,\dots,m
, by their expressions in terms of the coordinates
t_{s},t_{2m+1-s}
.

\square

Theorem 6.2

Let
\phi_{j}
be the Abelian differential of the third kind
\phi_{j}=\Omega_{0a_{j}}
(
6.2
),
j=1,\dots,m
. Then the corresponding prepotential is given by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{j}}=\frac{x_{j,j}}{2}\big(x_{j,2m+1-j}\big)^{2}+\big(x_{j,2m+1-j}\big)\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-k}x_{j,k}+e^{x_{j,j}}+\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-k}\Big(e^{x_{j,k}}-e^{x_{j,j}-x_{j,k}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}\big(x_{j,2m+1-k}\big)^{2}\Big(x_{j,k}+\log(x_{j,2m+1-k})\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}\sum_{s=1,s\neq j,k}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-k}x_{j,2m+1-s}\log\big(e^{x_{j,s}}-e^{x_{j,k}}\big).

It satisfies:

\mathbf{E}_{\phi_{j}}.\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{j}}=2\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{j}}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\big(x_{j,2m+1-k}\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k,s=1,k\neq s}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-k}x_{j,2m+1-s},

with
\mathbf{E}_{\phi_{j}}
being the Euler vector field (
6.14
).

Proof:
Applying formula (
6.17
) and using the fact that
{\rm deg}(\phi_{j})=0
and

E.x_{j,r}=E.\mathbf{t}^{r}(\phi_{j})=1+\delta_{jr},\quad\quad E.x_{j,2m+1-r}=x_{j,2m+1-r},\quad\forall\ r=1,\dots,m,

we can write

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{j}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k,s=1}^{m}\big(1+\delta_{jk}\big)\big(1+\delta_{js}\big)y_{k,2m+1-s}+\frac{3}{4}\sum_{k,s=1}^{m}\big(1+\delta_{js}\big)x_{j,2m+1-k}x_{k,2m+1-s}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k,s=1}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-k}x_{j,2m+1-s}x_{k,s}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\bigg(\sum_{s=1}^{m}y_{k,2m+1-s}\bigg)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{s=1}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-s}+\frac{1}{4}y_{j,2m+1-j}+\frac{3}{4}\sum_{k=1}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-k}\bigg(x_{k,2m+1-j}+\sum_{s=1}^{m}x_{k,2m+1-s}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{x_{j,j}}{2}\big(x_{j,2m+1-j}\big)^{2}+\big(x_{j,2m+1-j}\big)\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-k}\Big(x_{j,k}+\log(-1)/2\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-k}\bigg(x_{j,2m+1-k}x_{k,k}+\sum_{s=1,s\neq j,k}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-s}x_{k,s}\bigg).

Thus the crucial part remaining to do is to express the coordinates
y_{k,2m+1-s}
,
y_{k,s}
and
x_{k,s}
by means of
x_{j,r}
and
x_{j,2m+1-r}
,
r=1,\dots,m
.

Employing (
6.8
), (
6.9
), (
6.11
) and (
6.12
), we deduce the following:

\bullet
When
j,k,s
are distinct, then

x_{k,s}=\log(e^{t_{s}}-e^{t_{k}}\big)=\log\big(e^{x_{j,s}}-e^{x_{j,k}}\big).

\bullet
For any
k\neq j
, we have

\displaystyle x_{k,k}=\log(x_{j,2m+1-k})+x_{j,k};

\displaystyle x_{k,2m+1-j}=-e^{x_{j,j}-x_{j,k}};

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{m}x_{k,2m+1-s}=\bigg(\sum_{s=1}^{m}x_{k,2m+1-s}-\sum_{s=1}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-s}\bigg)+\sum_{s=1}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-s}=e^{x_{j,k}}+\sum_{s=1}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-s};

\displaystyle y_{j,2m+1-k}=-x_{j,2m+1-k}x_{k,2m+1-j}=x_{j,2m+1-k}e^{x_{j,j}-x_{j,k}}.

\bullet
For all
k=1,\dots,m
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{m}y_{k,2m+1-s}=e^{x_{k,k}}=\delta_{jk}e^{x_{j,j}}+(1-\delta_{jk})x_{j,2m+1-k}e^{x_{j,k}};

\displaystyle y_{j,2m+1-j}=\sum_{s=1}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-s}-\sum_{s=1,s\neq j}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-s}=e^{x_{j,j}}-\sum_{s=1,s\neq j}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-s}e^{x_{j,j}-x_{j,s}}.

Substituting these relations into the previous expression of
\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{j}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{j}}=\frac{1}{4}e^{x_{j,j}}+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-k}e^{x_{j,k}}+\frac{1}{2}e^{x_{j,j}}+\frac{1}{4}e^{x_{j,j}}-\frac{1}{4}\sum_{s=1,s\neq j}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-s}e^{x_{j,j}-x_{j,s}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{3}{4}x_{j,2m+1-j}\bigg(x_{j,2m+1-j}+\sum_{s=1}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-s}\bigg)+\frac{3}{4}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-k}\bigg(-e^{x_{j,j}-x_{j,k}}+e^{x_{j,k}}+\sum_{s=1}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-s}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{x_{j,j}}{2}\big(x_{j,2m+1-j}\big)^{2}+\big(x_{j,2m+1-j}\big)\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-k}\Big(x_{j,k}+\frac{\log(-1)}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}\big(x_{j,2m+1-k}\big)^{2}\Big(x_{j,k}+\log(x_{j,2m+1-k})\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}\sum_{s=1,s\neq j,k}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-k}x_{j,2m+1-s}\log\big(e^{x_{j,s}}-e^{x_{j,k}}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{j,j}}{2}\big(x_{j,2m+1-j}\big)^{2}+\big(x_{j,2m+1-j}\big)\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-k}x_{j,k}+e^{x_{j,j}}+\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-k}\Big(e^{x_{j,k}}-e^{x_{j,j}-x_{j,k}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}\big(x_{j,2m+1-k}\big)^{2}\Big(x_{j,k}+\log(x_{j,2m+1-k})\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}\sum_{s=1,s\neq j,k}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-k}x_{j,2m+1-s}\log\big(e^{x_{j,s}}-e^{x_{j,k}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\log(-1)}{2}\big(x_{j,2m+1-j}\big)\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-k}+\frac{3}{4}\big(x_{j,2m+1-j}\big)^{2}+\frac{3}{4}\sum_{k,s=1}^{m}x_{j,2m+1-k}x_{j,2m+1-s}.

Finally, subtracting the quadratic terms we arrive at the stated expression for the function
\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{j}}
.

\square

Theorem 6.3

For
j=1,\dots,m
, let
\phi_{2m+1-j}
be the Abelian differential (
6.2
). Then the prepotential associated with the semi-simple Frobenius manifold structure induced by the differential
\phi_{2m+1-j}
is determined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{2m+1-j}}=\frac{\big(y_{jj}\big)^{2}}{2}y_{j,2m+1-j}+y_{jj}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}y_{j,k}y_{j,2m+1-k}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}\big(y_{j,k}\big)^{2}y_{j,2m+1-k}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-k}\bigg)^{2}\log\Big(\sum_{r=1}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-r}\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}\big(y_{j,2m+1-k}\big)^{2}\log\big(y_{j,2m+1-k}\big)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{k,s=1,s\neq j}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-k}y_{j,2m+1-s}\log(y_{j,s})+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}\sum_{s=1,s\neq j,k}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-k}y_{j,2m+1-s}\log\big(y_{j,s}-y_{j,k}\big)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{12}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}\sum_{s=1,s\neq j,k}^{m}\frac{y_{j,k}y_{j,2m+1-k}y_{j,2m+1-s}}{y_{j,k}-y_{j,s}}.

The function
\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{2m+1-j}}
is quasi-homogeneous of degree 4 with respect to the Euler vector field (
6.15
) and we have

\mathbf{E}_{\phi_{2m+1-j}}.\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{2m+1-j}}=4\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{2m+1-j}}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\big(y_{j,2m+1-k}\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k,s=1,s\neq k}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-k}y_{j,2m+1-s}.

Proof:
Since
{\rm deg}(\phi_{2m+1-j})=1
, formulas (
6.17
) and (
6.15
) imply that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{2m+1-j}}=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{k,s=1}^{m}y_{j,k}y_{j,s}y_{k,2m+1-s}+\frac{5}{12}\sum_{k,s=1}^{m}y_{j,s}y_{j,2m+1-k}y_{s,k}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k,s=1}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-k}y_{j,2m+1-s}x_{k,s}

\displaystyle=\frac{\big(y_{jj}\big)^{2}}{12}y_{j,2m+1-j}+\frac{y_{jj}}{6}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}y_{j,k}y_{j,2m+1-k}+\frac{1}{12}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}\bigg((y_{j,k})^{2}y_{k,2m+1-k}+y_{j,k}\sum_{s=1,s\neq j,k}^{m}y_{j,s}y_{k,2m+1-s}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{5}{12}\big(y_{j,j}\big)^{2}y_{j,2m+1-j}+\frac{5}{12}y_{j,2m+1-j}\sum_{s=1,s\neq j}^{m}y_{j,s}y_{s,j}+\frac{5}{6}y_{j,j}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-k}y_{j,k}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{5}{12}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-k}\bigg(y_{j,k}\big(y_{k,k}-y_{jj}\big)+\sum_{s=1,s\neq j,k}^{m}y_{j,s}y_{s,k}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\big(y_{j,2m+1-j}\big)^{2}x_{j,j}+y_{j,2m+1-j}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-k}\Big(x_{k,j}+\frac{\log(-1)}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}\bigg((y_{j,2m+1-k})^{2}x_{k,k}+y_{j,2m+1-k}\sum_{s=1,s\neq j,k}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-s}x_{k,s}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{\big(y_{jj}\big)^{2}}{2}y_{j,2m+1-j}+y_{jj}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}y_{j,k}y_{j,2m+1-k}+\frac{1}{12}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}\bigg(\underbrace{(y_{j,k})^{2}y_{k,2m+1-k}+y_{j,k}\sum_{s=1,s\neq j,k}^{m}y_{j,s}y_{k,2m+1-s}}_{Q_{j,k}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{5}{12}y_{j,2m+1-j}\sum_{s=1,s\neq j}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-s}+\frac{5}{12}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-k}\bigg(\underbrace{y_{j,k}\big(y_{k,k}-y_{jj}\big)+\sum_{s=1,s\neq j,k}^{m}y_{j,s}y_{s,k}}_{R_{j,k}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\big(y_{j,2m+1-j}\big)^{2}x_{j,j}+\frac{\log(-1)}{2}y_{j,2m+1-j}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-k}+y_{j,2m+1-j}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-k}x_{k,j}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}\bigg((y_{j,2m+1-k})^{2}x_{k,k}+y_{j,2m+1-k}\sum_{s=1,s\neq j,k}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-s}x_{k,s}\bigg).

As above, we need some intermediate results to complete the calculation.

i)

For all
k\neq j
we have

\displaystyle y_{k,j}=-\frac{y_{j,2m+1-k}}{y_{j,k}};

\displaystyle x_{k,j}=x_{j,j}-\log(y_{j,k});

\displaystyle x_{k,k}=x_{j,j}+\log(y_{j,2m+1-k})-2\log(y_{j,k}).

ii)

If
j,k,s
are distinct, then

\displaystyle x_{k,s}=x_{j,j}+\log\big(y_{j,s}-y_{j,k}\big)-\log(y_{j,k})-\log(y_{j,s});

\displaystyle y_{k,s}=e^{x_{k,k}-x_{s,k}}=\frac{y_{j,2m+1-k}y_{j,s}}{y_{j,k}\big(y_{j,k}-y_{j,s}\big)};

\displaystyle y_{k,2m+1-s}=-y_{k,s}y_{s,k}=\frac{y_{j,2m+1-k}y_{j,2m+1-s}}{\big(y_{j,k}-y_{j,s}\big)^{2}}.

To see these equalities we use expressions (
6.8
) and (
6.9
) involving the variables
t_{k},t_{m+k}
.

In addition, items
i)
and
ii)
and relations (
6.11
) and (
6.12
) lead to the following required assertions:

iii)

For all
k\neq j
:

\displaystyle Q_{j,k}

\displaystyle:=(y_{j,k})^{2}y_{k,2m+1-k}+y_{j,k}\sum_{s=1,s\neq j,k}^{m}y_{j,s}y_{k,2m+1-s}

\displaystyle=(y_{j,k})^{2}e^{x_{k,k}}-(y_{j,k})^{2}\sum_{s=1,s\neq k}^{m}y_{k,2m+1-s}+y_{j,k}\sum_{s=1,s\neq j,k}^{m}y_{j,s}y_{k,2m+1-s}

\displaystyle=y_{j,2m+1-k}e^{x_{j,j}}-(y_{j,k})^{2}y_{j,2m+1-k}-\sum_{s=1,s\neq j,k}^{m}\frac{y_{j,k}y_{j,2m+1-k}y_{j,2m+1-s}}{y_{j,k}-y_{j,s}}.

vi)

For all
k\neq j
:

\displaystyle R_{j,k}

\displaystyle=y_{j,k}\big(y_{k,k}-y_{j,j}\big)+\sum_{s=1,s\neq j,k}^{m}y_{j,s}y_{s,k}

\displaystyle=y_{j,k}\bigg(-\frac{e^{x_{j,j}}}{y_{j,k}}+\sum_{s=1,s\neq j}^{m}y_{s,j}-\sum_{s=1,s\neq k}^{m}y_{s,k}\bigg)+\sum_{s=1,s\neq j,k}^{m}y_{j,s}y_{s,k}

\displaystyle=-e^{x_{j,j}}-y_{j,k}\sum_{s=1,s\neq j}^{m}\frac{y_{j,2m+1-s}}{y_{j,s}}-\big(y_{j,k}\big)^{2}+\sum_{s=1,s\neq j,k}^{m}\big(y_{j,s}-y_{j,k}\big)y_{s,k}

\displaystyle=-e^{x_{j,j}}-y_{j,2m+1-k}-\big(y_{j,k}\big)^{2}.

Finally, omitting all the quadratic terms and employing relations indicated in items
i)
-
vi)
and recalling that due to (
6.12
) we have
e^{x_{j,j}}=\sum_{r=1}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-r}
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{2m+1-j}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\big(y_{jj}\big)^{2}}{2}y_{j,2m+1-j}+y_{jj}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}y_{j,k}y_{j,2m+1-k}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{12}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}\bigg(y_{j,2m+1-k}e^{x_{j,j}}-(y_{j,k})^{2}y_{j,2m+1-k}-\sum_{s=1,s\neq j,k}^{m}\frac{y_{j,k}y_{j,2m+1-k}y_{j,2m+1-s}}{y_{j,k}-y_{j,s}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{5}{12}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-k}\bigg(-e^{x_{j,j}}-y_{j,2m+1-k}-\big(y_{j,k}\big)^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\big(y_{j,2m+1-j}\big)^{2}x_{j,j}+y_{j,2m+1-j}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-k}\Big(x_{j,j}-\log(y_{j,k})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}\big(y_{j,2m+1-k}\big)^{2}\bigg(x_{j,j}+\log\big(y_{j,2m+1-k}\big)-2\log(y_{j,k})\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1,k\neq j}^{m}\sum_{s=1,s\neq j,k}^{m}y_{j,2m+1-k}y_{j,2m+1-s}\bigg(x_{j,j}+\log\big(y_{j,s}-y_{j,k}\big)-\log\big(y_{j,s}\big)-\log\big(y_{j,k}\big)\bigg)

which is equivalent to the expression claimed in the theorem.

\square

Remark 6.1

Let us consider the case
m=2
of the obtained solutions to the WDVV equations stated in the above three theorems.

For
j=1,2
, we introduce

\begin{array}[]{cccc}x_{1}=x_{j,j},&x_{2}=x_{j,k},\quad j\neq k,&x_{3}=x_{j,5-k},\quad k\neq j,&x_{4}=x_{j,5-j}\\
\\
y_{1}=y_{j,j},&y_{2}=y_{j,k},\quad j\neq k,&y_{3}=y_{j,5-k},\quad k\neq j,&y_{4}=y_{j,5-j}\\
\end{array}

Then we have

\displaystyle F_{1}

\displaystyle=t_{4}e^{t_{1}}+t_{3}e^{t_{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\Big(t_{4}^{2}t_{1}+t_{4}^{2}\log(t_{4})+t_{3}^{2}t_{2}+t_{3}^{2}\log(t_{3})\Big)+t_{3}t_{4}\log\big(e^{t_{1}}-e^{t_{2}}\big)+\frac{\log(-1)}{2}t_{3}t_{4};

\displaystyle F_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{1}}{2}x_{4}^{2}+x_{2}x_{3}x_{4}+e^{x_{1}}+x_{3}e^{x_{2}}-x_{3}e^{x_{1}-x_{2}}+\frac{x_{3}^{2}}{2}x_{2}+\frac{x_{3}^{2}}{2}\log(x_{3});

\displaystyle F_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{y_{1}^{2}}{2}y_{4}+y_{1}y_{2}y_{3}-\frac{y_{2}^{2}}{2}y_{3}+\frac{1}{2}(y_{3}+y_{4})^{2}\log\big(y_{3}+y_{4}\big)+\frac{y_{3}^{2}}{2}\log(y_{3})-(y_{3}+y_{4})y_{3}\log(y_{2}).

7
Examples of genus one solutions to the WDVV equations

7.1
Weierstrass functions

We begin with recalling some useful formulas involving the three Weierstrass functions
\wp,\zeta
and
\sigma
associated with the lattice
\mathbb{L}=2\omega_{1}\mathbb{Z}+2\omega_{2}\mathbb{Z}
, where
\omega_{1},\omega_{2}
are two complex numbers chosen in such a manner that the imaginary part of the ratio
\tau:=\omega_{2}/{\omega_{1}}
is positive. For more details on the properties of Weierstrass functions and related topics we refer to the monographs
[
2
,
3
,
6
,
8
,
20
,
24
,
43
]
.

The functions
\wp
,
\zeta
and
\sigma
are respectively defined by:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\wp(u)=\wp(u;2\omega_{1},2\omega_{2}):=\frac{1}{u^{2}}+\sum_{w\in\mathbb{L},w\neq 0}\Big(\frac{1}{(u-w)^{2}}-\frac{1}{w^{2}}\Big);\\
&\zeta(u)=\zeta(u;2\omega_{1},2\omega_{2}):=\frac{1}{u}+\sum_{w\in\mathbb{L},w\neq 0}\Big(\frac{1}{u-w}+\frac{1}{w}+\frac{u}{w^{2}}\Big);\\
&\sigma(u)=\sigma(u;2\omega_{1},2\omega_{2}):=u\prod_{w\in\mathbb{L},w\neq 0}\big(1-\frac{u}{w}\big)\exp\Big(\frac{u}{w}+\frac{u^{2}}{2w^{2}}\Big).\end{split}

(7.1)

Here is the list of properties of the three functions that will be important in what follows.

1.

Differential equations for the
\wp
-function:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\wp^{\prime 2}(u)=4\wp^{3}(u)-g_{2}\wp(u)-g_{3}=4\big(\wp(u)-e_{1}\big)\big(\wp(u)-e_{2}\big)\big(\wp(u)-e_{3}\big);\end{split}

(7.2)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\wp^{\prime\prime}(u)=6\wp^{2}(u)-\frac{g_{2}}{2};\\
&\wp^{\prime\prime\prime}(u)=12\wp^{\prime}(u)\wp(u);\\
&\wp^{(4)}(u)=120\wp^{3}(u)-18g_{2}\wp(u)-12g_{3},\end{split}

(7.3)

where
\omega_{3}:=\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}
,
e_{j}=\wp(\omega_{j})
and

g_{2}:=g_{2}(2\omega_{1},2\omega_{2}):=60\sum_{w\in\mathbb{L},w\neq 0}\frac{1}{w^{4}},\quad\quad\quad g_{3}:=g_{3}(2\omega_{1},2\omega_{2}):=140\sum_{w\in\mathbb{L},w\neq 0}\frac{1}{w^{6}}.

(7.4)

2.

The
\zeta
-Weierstrass function satisfies:

\zeta^{\prime}(u):=-\wp(u).

(7.5)

3.

The function
\zeta
is the logarithmic derivative of
\sigma
:

\sigma^{\prime}(u)/\sigma(u)=\zeta(u).

(7.6)

4.

We have the homogeneity relations for arbitrary
\alpha\neq 0
:

\begin{split}&\wp(\alpha u;2\alpha\omega_{1},2\alpha\omega_{2})=\alpha^{-2}\wp(u;2\omega_{1},2\omega_{2});\\
&\zeta(\alpha u;2\alpha\omega_{1},2\alpha\omega_{2})=\alpha^{-1}\zeta(u;2\omega_{1},2\omega_{2});\\
&\sigma(\alpha u;2\alpha\omega_{1},2\alpha\omega_{2})=\alpha\sigma(u;2\omega_{1},2\omega_{2}).\end{split}

(7.7)

5.

The functions
\zeta
and
\sigma
satisfy the following quasi-periodicity properties:

\zeta(u+2\omega_{j})=\zeta(u)+2\zeta(\omega_{j}),\quad\sigma(u+2\omega_{j})=-\sigma(u)e^{2\zeta(\omega_{j})u+2\omega_{j}\zeta(\omega_{j})}.

(7.8)

6.

The periods
\omega_{1},\omega_{2}
and the quasi-periods of
\zeta
are related by the Legendre relation:

\omega_{2}\zeta(\omega_{1})-\omega_{1}\zeta(\omega_{2})=\frac{{\rm{i}}\pi}{2}.

(7.9)

7.

Addition theorems for the functions
\wp
and
\zeta
:

\displaystyle\wp(u+v)=-\wp(u)-\wp(v)+\frac{1}{4}\bigg(\frac{\wp^{\prime}(u)-\wp^{\prime}(v)}{\wp(u)-\wp(v)}\bigg)^{2};

(7.10)

\displaystyle\zeta(u+v)=\zeta(u)+\zeta(v)+\frac{\wp^{\prime}(u)-\wp^{\prime}(v)}{2\big(\wp(u)-\wp(v)\big)}.

(7.11)

7.2
Examples of 3-dimensional Hurwitz-Frobenius manifolds and Ramanujan identities

We are interested in some examples of Frobenius manifold structures on the Hurwitz space
\mathcal{H}_{1,2}(1)
of genus one two-fold ramified coverings
(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{L},\lambda)
of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
where
\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{L}
is the torus associated with the lattice
\mathbb{L}=2\omega_{1}\mathbb{Z}+2\omega_{2}\mathbb{Z}
and
\lambda
is the meromorphic function given by

\lambda(P)=\wp(z_{P})+c,\quad P\in\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{L}.

(7.12)

Here
c
is a nonzero constant (with respect to
z_{P}
) and
z_{P}
is a representative of the point
P
in the fundamental parallelogram

\mathcal{F}:=\Big\{2\alpha\omega_{1}+2\beta\omega_{2}:\quad 0\leq\alpha,\beta\leq 1\Big\}.

Due to the algebraic differential equation (
7.2
), the covering
(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{L},\lambda)
can be identified with the elliptic curve (known as the Weierstrass elliptic curve):

\Big\{P=(\lambda,\mu):\quad\mu^{2}=4(\lambda-\lambda_{1})(\lambda-\lambda_{2})(\lambda-\lambda_{3})\Big\},

where
\lambda_{j}=\wp(\omega_{j})+c=e_{j}+c
and
\mu=\wp^{\prime}(z_{P})
.
It has four simple ramification points
P_{j}=(\lambda_{j},0)
for
j=1,2,3
and
\infty^{0}=(\infty,\infty)
. Note that the parameter
c
makes the branch points
\lambda_{j}
independent of each other (if
c=0
, they become dependent since
e_{1}+e_{2}+e_{3}=0
) and this permits us to use them as local coordinates on the
Hurwitz space
\mathcal{H}_{1,2}(1)
.

Using the homogeneity relation (
7.7
) we can see that the equivalence class (as defined in Section 2.2) of the pair
(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{L},\lambda)
consists of ramified coverings
(\mathbb{C}/\widetilde{\mathbb{L}},\widetilde{\lambda})
such that

\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}=\alpha\mathbb{L}\quad\quad\text{and}\quad\quad\widetilde{\lambda}(P)=\alpha^{2}\wp(z_{P},2\alpha\omega_{1},2\alpha\omega_{2})+c

for some nonzero complex number
\alpha
.

In order to construct Hurwitz-Frobenius manifold structures in genus one, we restrict ourselves to an open neighborhood in
\mathcal{H}_{1,2}(1)
of the generic covering
(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{L},\lambda)
with a fixed Torelli marking
\{a,b\}
. Here the cycles
a,b
are fixed to be the segments
a=[x,x+2\omega_{1}]
and
b=[x,x+2\omega_{2}]
.

We will apply formula (
4.31
) to compute the prepotentials of the semi-simple Hurwitz-Frobenius manifolds structures determined by the following differentials:

\displaystyle\phi_{1}(P)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2{\rm{i}}\pi}\oint_{b}W(P,Q);

\displaystyle\phi_{2}(P)

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{2}{\rm res}_{\infty^{0}}\sqrt{\lambda(Q)}W(P,Q);

\displaystyle\phi_{3}(P)

\displaystyle:=\oint_{a}\lambda(Q)W(P,Q).

According to (
3.23
) and Corollary
4.1
, the three differentials
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
and
\phi_{3}
are primary with respect to the unit vector field
e=\sum_{j=1}^{3}\partial_{\lambda_{j}}
. Furthermore, the differentials
\phi_{j}
are all quasi-homogeneous and their quasi-homogeneous degrees are:

deg(\phi_{1})=0,\quad deg(\phi_{2})=1/2,\quad deg(\phi_{3})=deg(\phi_{4})=1.

As a first step, we start with the following explicit formula for the symmetric bidifferential
W
(see Section 2.3) in genus one:

Lemma 7.1

The canonical meromorphic bidifferential on the torus
\mathbb{C}/{\mathbb{L}}
, equipped with a fixed canonical homology basis
\{a,b\}
, is given by

W(P,Q)=\Big(\wp(z_{P}-z_{Q})+\frac{\zeta(\omega_{1})}{\omega_{1}}\Big)dz_{P}dz_{Q}.

(7.13)

Proof:
The right hand side is clearly a symmetric bidifferential and has a second-order pole on the diagonal
P=Q
with biresidue 1. Moreover, due to (
7.5
